## The Backlog Directive — restated in full

> **This file is the list of outstanding activities for Phase 2 (Engine & Systems).** The *how-we-work* rules live in **[CONTRIBUTING.md](../../CONTRIBUTING.md)**; closed/shipped work is archived in **[plan-archive.md](plan-archive.md)**. Split out of the former `plan.md` on 2026-07-09.
>
> **⚠️ Before starting ANY row — Existing-Work-First (CONTRIBUTING.md § Existing-Work-First Policy):** `git status` + `git log` first (uncommitted/fresh work matching the row = it's done or in progress — finish it, don't redo it); check for a second live session (`ps aux | grep claude`) before editing shared files; grep to *disprove* the row's premise (rows go stale — four rows have closed as ALREADY SHIPPED). A §RESUME entry says where the last session *stopped*, not what the tree looks like *now*.
>
> **Legend:** `[ ]` open · `[~]` in-progress / recurring · `[x]` closed (kept here for context; migrate to plan-archive.md over time).

> **⚠️ This file is one of six.** A row's neighbours may live in a sibling phase file, and the Existing-Work-First grep MUST cover all six — `grep -n '§DX-02xx' BACKLOG*.md` — before you conclude a row is unclaimed. The §RESUME entry that closed a row may sit in a different phase file than the row itself.

## Standing Directive — Code Comments (user directive 2026-08-23)

> **Binding on every increment in this file.** Where this and a §-row's own instructions disagree, this wins.

**The failure this prevents:** writing a comment that narrates the change being made right now. That context is real, but it expires the instant the change merges — the defect it describes no longer exists, so the comment becomes a story about a problem no future reader can observe. It is a changelog entry in the wrong file, and a third copy of text already required in the commit body and the PR description.

- **CC-1 (MUST NOT)** Write a comment describing a change, a fix, a defect, its cause, or what the code used to do. No *"was / now / previously / instead of"*, no *"this fixes"*, no *"needed because otherwise"*, no *"note that we no longer"*.
- **CC-2 (MUST)** Apply the **survival test** to every comment before writing it: *would this still be true and useful to someone reading this file a year from now, who never saw the diff?* If it only makes sense beside the diff, it is changelog — delete it and put it in the commit body.
- **CC-3 (MUST)** Default to **zero** comments. Declarative config — Terraform, DNS records, k8s manifests, CI YAML, Helm values — is self-describing and takes none. A resource named `dmarc-example-com` does not need a comment saying it is the DMARC record.
- **CC-4 (MAY)** Comment only when a future editor would actively break something without it: a non-obvious external constraint, a required out-of-band manual step, an invariant the surrounding code cannot show. **One line.** If it needs a paragraph it belongs in a design doc, not inline.
- **CC-5 (MUST)** Before every commit, re-read the comment lines added: `git diff --cached | grep '^+' | grep -E '#|//|/\*'`. Each hit must pass CC-2 on its own. **Deleting is always an acceptable outcome.** *"I already wrote it"*, *"it is only one line"*, and *"this one is genuinely useful"* are not exemptions — the last one is the exact thought that precedes every violation.
- **CC-6 (MUST)** Applies to comments **edited** as well as added. When a change invalidates an existing comment, the default action is **DELETE**, not rewrite it into a new narrative.

> **⚠️ KNOWN TENSION, flagged not resolved — needs a user call.** This repo currently runs an *opposite* convention in `play.html`: **§AUDIT-03n / §DX-02c "annotate, don't rewrite"**, which deliberately preserves defect history inline (e.g. `const NODE_NPC_KEYS`'s 8-line comment explaining that 21 of 26 rows named retired node codes, or `_renderFinalMap`'s §AUDIT-03e note on why the node filter is held at the historical set). Those are exactly the *"what the code used to do"* narratives CC-1 forbids, and several are load-bearing: they are the only record of why a value is held where it is, and deleting them would re-open closed audit rows. **Until the call is made, the working rule is:** CC-1..CC-6 bind all **new** comments; existing §AUDIT-/§DX-annotated blocks are **not** swept under CC-6 — they are a documented archaeology convention, not accidental changelog. Filed as **§DX-02fa** below.

## §BACKLOG — Open Items (Phase 2)

### §POT-PROMOTE — potential.md Seed Batch → 12 Tracks (⚠️ PLANNED 2026-07-23)

> **Status: ⚠️ PLANNED — specs only, no HTML edited.** This batch promotes the **16 OPEN + unblocked seeds** from `potential.md` (the ones that need neither §VM-01 nor a load-bearing constraint broken) into **12 coherent `§`-tracks**, merging where `potential.md` itself says the work is one surface (W1+S4+M1 → one codex; W4+W5+P2 → the shipped `textVariants` rail). The **BLOCKED seeds stay in `potential.md` by design** — C5/H4/B4/R1/R3/S2 need **§VM-01 Inc A** (the `choice`/`wait` opcode) and X3 needs **§VM-01-F** (the gate expression evaluator); promoting one would grow a private branch mechanism, exactly the *"new single-use term"* the **Host/Script Separation Policy** forbids. `potential.md` §POT-C2's actionable half already shipped as **§NPC-01-C** (`meta.enemy` ⚔ footer); only its faction-response half remains, blocked. §POT-C3 became **§NPC-01-SF5** above (the drift re-scoped it into the NPC-card track).
>
> **Evidence standard.** Every anchor below was **re-measured live 2026-07-23** at `ENGINE_VER = 'coc-3.104.0'` (`play.html`, **37,870 lines**), not carried from `potential.md`'s `43bd09c` snapshot — several of that snapshot's claims had already drifted (C2/C3 surfaces shipped via §NPC-01; `worldTruth` now renders at Dear Friend). **Re-grep every anchor before editing anyway** — line numbers are hints, they drift (§DX-01e). **Each track is a spec, not code:** build one increment, verify, mark it, stop. A track that grows to a chain/system/arc gets a `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-*.md` **before any HTML edit** (Lab Report Policy). ⚠️ Restart the WBAPI server before any write session (Hazard #1). *(`api.sh post monster` — the old Hazard #2 — was fixed 2026-07-30 by §DX-01c and is now the normal path.)*

#### §CODEX-01 — The read-only Codex: surface what the engine already knows (merges §POT-W1 + §POT-S4 + §POT-M1) 🔴

**The finding (measured).** The game has **no rules surface of any kind** — `grep -ci "helpScreen|rulesTab|glossary|codexScreen|showRules|showHelp"` = **0** across 37,870 lines. The entire tutorial is **one NPC monologue** (`yael.impartial[0]`), and §PLAY-01-D had to add a special-case guarantee (`yaelOnboardingSeen`) purely to deliver it once because the auto-active Slums quest shadowed it. **(§DOC-02ch 2026-08-18 measured that monologue: 2,228 characters / 426 words, the longest single string a new player is shown; the fishing signpost §PLAY-01-D added to it begins at character 1,089 — word 206 of 426, the exact midpoint. The content was corrected and the delivery vehicle was not, so any future signpost bolted onto this speech lands in the same place. That number is the argument for this track.)** Meanwhile the engine privately knows a lot it never transmits: `_magicTierAllowed` = `level ≥ magic×5`, `XP_LEVELS`, `CONDITION_ADV`, `_monsterLevel`, `EFFORT_XP_PCT` (`const EFFORT_XP_PCT@24427`), the d100 loot weights. Two neighbours have the same shape: **downtime verbs exist with no hub** (`downtime` = 0 hits, but fishing with 20 ranks + `FISHING_GUIDE_TEXT` (`const FISHING_GUIDE_TEXT@26660`), 5 vendor nodes, **38** `sleep:true` rest nodes, Hunt Mode, 12 `type:'craft'` quests all ship — the game never tells you a place *offers* them); and **world lore lives only as prose** — `WORLD_DB` (`const WORLD_DB@6279`) is `{label,icon,monsters[]}` with no political/historical field, and **219 `meta.worldTruth` lines** now render only as the §NPC-01-C `✦` footer at Dear Friend (fav≥2), so most are still unread at any reachable favor.

**The thesis (why one surface, not three).** This is the **textbook §PLAY-01 "honesty" class** — *the engine knows things it won't transmit* — the exact pattern §PLAY-01-A proved safe (the objective chip: surface the known, diegetically, UI-only, **zero balance risk, no design call**). `potential.md` §POT-M1 explicitly says *"Merge with §POT-W1 + §POT-S4 into one codex surface."* Do **not** add `culture:`/`power:` fields to `WORLD_DB` first — that is authoring, and the content already exists unread; this track is a **renderer**, not new lore.

**Increments (each a shippable slice):**
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-A — the Codex shell + Rules/Glossary tab (keystone).** A read-only overlay (mirror the objective-chip / void-modal UI patterns) with a Rules pane that reads the engine's *own* constants live — skill-check formula, magic-tier rule, XP curve, effort-XP %, loot weights, `TIER_LABELS`. No new data: it reflects existing constants so it can never drift from balance.
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-B — Gazetteer pane (folds §POT-W1).** Per-region lore aggregated from already-authored strings — `NODE_MAP[].text` + the region's `worldTruth` lines — as a browsable read-only index. Still a renderer; authors nothing.
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-C — "What this place offers" downtime board (folds §POT-S4).** At the current node, list the verbs that actually apply here (rest + cost, vendor, fishing if a rod-node, craft, Hunt) by querying node/state — the honest "you have time; here's what's here" surface §POT-S4 wants.

**Invariants:** render-only — **Free-Movement untouched** (no mover code, no quest-state in a step); outside all four parity fences (no `src/js/*.js` twin — inline engine JS, edited directly); **seeded-RNG N/A** (no rolls). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline-JS hand-edit — stop the server first, commit early). **Verify:** new `src/tests/integration/codex.test.js` (overlay opens/closes; Rules pane reflects the live constants; Gazetteer lists ≥1 `worldTruth`; downtime board matches the node's real verbs) + eyeball (§7½). Baseline reds unchanged (I1/I2, R2/R3).

#### §TIDE-01 — The world visibly changes after events (merges §POT-P2 + §POT-W5 + §POT-W4) 🔴

**The finding (measured).** §GR shipped `node.textVariants:[{flag,text}]` (`85faf9b`) — recorded as *"display-only, API-authored, **reusable for any 'node changes after an event'."*** The renderer is generic and idle: `_renderNodeText`-side loop at `node.textVariants@34594` (`for (const _v of (node.textVariants||[]))… if (S_story[_v.flag]) …`). Measured consumers: **exactly one** (`flag:'fishmongerRowRestored'@8802`, the §GR payoff). So a **built, tested, API-authorable mechanism has 1 of 401 possible consumers.** Two beats want it and have never used it: **(W5) the apocalypse is narrated in past tense and never becomes world state** — `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS` (`const VOID_TIDE_EVENTS@22369`) narrate the Void's advance and `_addVoidPressure` (`function _addVoidPressure@26976`, called on sleep at `Increment void pressure at each tide event@36346`) advances a counter, but **no node has ever become ruined**; and **(W4) the 1367-vs-cyberpunk blend is unjustified in-world** — §1367 already fixed this class once (Events A–F were parked at the HKG cyberpunk node and had to be relocated to thematic nodes).

**The design (already legal, already implemented).** CONTRIBUTING's Free-Movement policy **explicitly blesses W5's shape**: *"If a future feature needs a place to feel impassable until something happens, it must do so by **changing terrain/`CELL_GRID`/`IMPASSABLE_CELLS` state** (the cell genuinely becomes/stops-being sea), not by consulting quest state inside the mover."* `IMPASSABLE_CELLS` is a computed set (`const IMPASSABLE_CELLS@9871`) → the Void tide rewrites *terrain/cell state* (the cell genuinely changes), never gates a step. W4/W5 node-text beats are **pure authoring against shipped rails** — `textVariants` needs **zero code**.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§TIDE-01-A — the W4 anachronism account (zero code) ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-23.** One `textVariants` beat giving the 1367-courier-in-Neon-Undercity blend a diegetic account, keyed on an existing flag. Pure `./api.sh` authoring; proves the idle renderer on a second consumer. **✅ Done:** added `textVariants:[{flag:'cyMaintenanceDecoded', text:…}]` to **HKG** (Neon Undercity, `8328`), keyed on the existing flag set by **`quest_d0207_a4`** ("The Cipher" — the moment the player decodes that a **Scholar King** built the Undercity three centuries ago, matching the Froberger cipher fragment). #### §XP-02 — First-arrival exploration XP (completes §XP-01) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** §XP-01's user directive was *"all action earns XP"* and it shipped **three** grant sites — fled enemy, missed attack, failed check (`EFFORT_XP_PCT = 0.25`, `const EFFORT_XP_PCT@24427`; fail-XP at `a failed check still earns effort XP@7003`). Yet **exploration — the one thing the game is built around — grants nothing.** `S_story.visited` is a bare boolean per node whose **only** consumer is map tinting (`_getNodeMapColor`, `function _getNodeMapColor@27563`; `.visited` = 17 refs, all cosmetic). Walking somewhere new earns nothing.

**The design.** This is an **unfinished §XP-01**, not new mechanics: add a fourth grant site keyed on *first* arrival at a node (guard on the existing `visited` boolean flipping false→true) using §XP-01's existing dials. **Bound it** so it can't out-earn combat (mirror §XP-01's cap: exploration XP ≤ the flee value) and so backtracking pays nothing (the `visited` guard already gives first-arrival-only for free). No seeded roll needed (a flat/level-scaled grant), so **RNG N/A**.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§XP-02-A — first-arrival grant ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-23.** On the `visited[node]` false→true transition, award a small bounded XP grant; make it level-aware if desired (a trivial node at L18 pays little) but keep it flat-simple first. **✅ Done:** new dial `const EXPLORE_XP = 10` (next to `EFFORT_XP_PCT`/`EFFORT_MISS_PCT`, `~24070`) + `_grantExplorationXp(node)` helper, hooked in `storyCollectLoot` (`~29655`, the sole `visited`-setting site) as `if (!S_story.visited[node.code]) _grantExplorationXp(node)` — **before** the visited flip, so it fires exactly once per node; backtracking pays nothing (the guard), and a `if (node.junction) return` guard skips routing junctions. XP is granted **inside `storyRender` after the free step already landed** (never gates movement); UI (message + any level-up modal) is **deferred via `setTimeout`** so the render finishes first, and `_levelUpQueue` is sliced-captured before the deferred read. Accrues telemetry to `S_story.explorationXp`. **Bounded** ≤ the weakest starter flee value (10 ≤ protofleder `round(12·16·0.25)`=48), so exploring can never out-earn a fight. **RNG N/A** (flat grant, `check:rng` green). **Verify:** new `exploration-xp.test.js` **2/2** (first arrival grants EXPLORE_XP once; re-visit grants 0; bounded ≤ protofleder flee; e2e telemetry through the real render path); inline `<script>` parse 0 errors. **Regression caught + fixed:** the full integration suite surfaced **1** real break — `quest-runtime-uqf.test.js:9509` (§MATH-01 "arrival at ZERO … xp+gold") calls `storyCollectLoot` on an unvisited node and asserted `xp===350+500`; now correctly `350+500+EXPLORE_XP` (the test was proven to be MY regression by passing on HEAD via stash-diff; the sibling arrival tests at `9538`/`9560` assert only gold/state, unaffected). **Full suite = 635 passed**; the 4 `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` + 1 flaky `multiplayer-presence` failures were **confirmed pre-existing** (identical on HEAD; worldbuilder tests never touch `play.html`). `check:walk` sub-checks parity/behaviour/terrain/roomsparity/questparity/gateast/rng/questgraph all **green**; only the **pre-existing** `check:invariants` (J14/J15 → §JUNK-01) + `check:roads` (R3 sea-overlap + 3 off-component settlements = the §DX-01 TGS/SPB baseline) stay red, both **unchanged** by this edit. Docs synced: `mechanics.md` §XP (three-dial table) + `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md`. Not committed (user rule).
- [ ] **§XP-02-B (optional) — a surfaced "unexplored" signal.** Pairs with §CODEX-01/§POT-H2's fog-of-war idea; defer unless wanted (no fog-of-war state today — do **not** add one speculatively).

**Invariants:** Free-Movement untouched (XP is granted *after* the free step lands, never gates it); outside all parity fences; if any randomness is ever added it draws the seeded stream (`_seededNext()`), never `Math.random()`. **Verify:** `src/tests/integration/exploration-xp.test.js` (first arrival grants once; a re-visit grants nothing; total exploration XP ≤ the §XP-01 cap) + `check:walk`. Sync `mechanics.md` §XP + `docs/mechanics/`.

#### §CLUE-01 — Cumulative multi-clue mysteries (no new opcode) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Mysteries are the file's best content (`quest_scar_01` "The Open Ledger"), but the shape is always **one `skill_check` → pass/fail → done** — no clue accumulation, no partial knowledge, because `execBits` is straight-line and cannot carry a result forward. **But the parts already ship:** `S_story.knowledge[]` is a clue store (the `reward` bit's `knowledge` field), and knowledge items already render — `.inv-item-knowledge` CSS exists (`.inv-item-knowledge@1546`, `.inv-item-knowledge@3238`). A **cumulative** mystery needs no VM change; only a **branching** one needs §VM-01.

**The design.** Author multi-clue mysteries as *existing* UQF: several quests each granting a `knowledge` clue, then a final quest gated `{all:[flags/knowledge…]}` (or `{any}`) that delivers the reveal `narrative`. **No new opcode, no `execBits` change** — this is a **content pattern demonstrated end-to-end**, not an engine track. ⚠️ Do **not** hand-author a *branching* chain: `skill_check` "smuggles its own branch" and CONTRIBUTING names it *"the exception to retire, not the pattern to copy."* Cumulative-only here.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§CLUE-01-A — confirm + document the pattern.** `./api.sh advise` on a scratch clue quest; write the canonical "cumulative mystery" recipe into `quest.md` (like the "collect at node" pattern) so future mysteries reuse it.
- [ ] **§CLUE-01-B — one exemplar arc.** Author a 3–4-clue mystery (clues via `knowledge` grants at distinct nodes → a `{all}`-gated reveal), UQF-only, with an integration test. Lab report only if it grows past a handful of quests.

**Invariants:** UQF data only — Host/Script separation kept (no new bit kind, no `_legacy_fn`); Free-Movement N/A (gates list missions, never block steps); seeded RNG for any check. **Verify:** `./api.sh advise` clean on each quest + `src/tests/integration/clue-mystery.test.js` (clues accumulate in `knowledge[]`; the reveal gate opens only when all are held) + `check:walk` (`check:questgraph` proves no soft-lock). Sync `quest.md`.

#### §BOSS-01 — Declarative boss phases + terrain-conditioned encounters (merges §POT-B3 + §POT-B2) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** `EPIC_BOSS_POOL` (10 refs) is **20 flat stat blocks** — no phases (`grep -c "phases:"` = **0**). The only staging is §PLAY-01-B's **one-time enrage at ≤30% HP**, tier-scaled, guarded by `S.opp.enraged` (reset `// §PLAY-01-B: one-time low-HP Void enrage@24640`, fire `S.opp.enraged = true@25212`). And **terrain is in the room but never in the fight**: no legendary/lair actions, no environmental advantage — combat reads terrain only via the `_VOID_TERRAIN_RE` name heuristic; the `ROOMS` kernel knows `terrainAt` but the fight never asks.

**The design (generalize shipped code into data).** Both halves have a **ready-made safe pattern**: §PLAY-01-B added its AI branch in `_storyEnemyTurn` (`function _storyEnemyTurn@25192`) — **outside the kernel** — leaving `DUEL:CORE` (`DUEL:CORE:START@10238`) git-diff-verified untouched. Do the same here.
- **B3 → `phases`:** a declarative `phases:[{atPct, atk, dmg, msg}]` field on `EPIC_BOSS_POOL` generalizes the enrage (which **is** a one-stage phase with working `S.opp.enraged` lifecycle) into data — squarely Host/Script-Separation-compliant (new shapes go in the grammar/data, never a new single-use term), reusing the enrage lifecycle rather than inventing one.
- **B2 → terrain-conditioned encounters:** read `terrainAt`/the node's terrain in `_storyEnemyTurn` (outside `DUEL:CORE`) to grant an environmental modifier, mirroring §PLAY-01-B's branch site.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§BOSS-01-A — `phases` field (B3).** Add the declarative field + a phase-transition check in the enrage site; migrate the existing enrage to be `phases:[{atPct:30,…}]` so current behavior is byte-identical, then author a 2-phase boss.
- [ ] **§BOSS-01-B — terrain modifier in `_storyEnemyTurn` (B2).** A small, data-described terrain→encounter effect, kept entirely outside the kernel.

**Invariants:** ⚠️ **`DUEL:CORE` (`DUEL:CORE:START@10238`) is parity-fenced** — never edit the inlined copy; the effect lives in `_storyEnemyTurn` (host, outside the fence); if `src/js/duel.js` is touched at all, re-inline + `src/scripts/check-duel-parity.js`. Enrage/phase rolls draw the **seeded** stream. Free-Movement N/A. **Verify:** `src/tests/integration/boss-phases.test.js` (enrage-as-phase byte-identical; a 2-phase boss transitions at each `atPct`; terrain modifier applies only on the right terrain) + `src/scripts/check-duel-parity.js` green (kernel untouched) + `check:walk`. Sync `monsters.md` (`EPIC_BOSS_POOL` `phases` shape) + `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md`.

#### §CHAR-01 — One-time point-buy at character creation ✅ ALREADY SHIPPED 2026-07-23 (the §POT-C1 open slice) 🟢

> **⚠️ CORRECTION (2026-07-23) — §CHAR-01-A was ALREADY SHIPPED; the "design call" is moot. grep-before-building win.** The finding below claimed `grep -c "pointBuy|standardArray|_ASI_LEVELS" = 0` and concluded "the player never chooses them." **That grep used the wrong terms.** The shipped point-buy is named `CC_BUDGET`/`_CC_COST`/`_cc_scores`/`_showCharCreate`/`story-charcreate-modal`. Measured live: a complete **27-point D&D point-buy** (`_CC_COST=[0,1,2,3,4,5,7,9]` for scores 8..15, `CC_BUDGET=27`, `37748`) in the **"⚔ Choose Your Origin"** modal (`4960`) with **custom + hard** origin tabs, +/- steppers clamped 8–15 with live budget enforcement (`_ccRefresh`/`_ccSpent`), **wired** `btn-continue-new`/`btn-gameover-new` → `_showCharCreate()` → `btn-charcreate-begin` → `storyNewGame(scores)` (`37816`) which writes `S_story.abilityScores` + derives `atkBonus`/`hpMax`. So the player DOES author the character the 2,631 checks are about. It just had **no test** — now locked by `char-creation.test.js` **4/4** (budget=27 + cost curve; modal + custom/hard tabs + begin button; custom Begin writes scores & derives ATK/HP; hard Begin ships the fixed array). **Same class as §GR/§DESIGN-03/§DUNGEON-01/§1367/§MATH-01 — "planned" work that was already live; grepping the real symbol names (not the seed's guessed ones) closed it.** Optional residual only: add an explicit **standard-array `[15,14,13,12,10,8]` preset** button as a third origin (convenience, not a fork) — logged as §CHAR-01-B below; the core one-time point-buy needs nothing.

**The finding (measured — SUPERSEDED by the correction above).** Fighter-only is **explicit design** (`FIGHTER_FEATURES`; the L10 entry: *"No style choice in this world — your body has become the weapon"*) and races/classes **must not** be built — they would invalidate the DC curve across 2,850 quests. **But** `abilityScores` (STR/DEX/CON/INT/WIS/CHA) drive **all 2,631 `skill_check` bits** via `_rollSkill` — the player DOES choose them at new game via the shipped point-buy (the original claim of "never chooses" was a wrong-search-term artifact, see correction).

**The design (honest, bounded, no rebalance).** A **one-time point-buy / standard-array at `storyNewGame()`** — no class, no race, **no DC rebalance** (total points fixed so the curve is unchanged in aggregate; a player merely *distributes* what was already implicitly flat). Fits `_S_DEFAULTS()` per §STATE-INIT (declare nothing new — the field exists; just let new-game write it). **Trap:** keep the point total balanced against the existing DC curve — this is redistribution, not power creep.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§CHAR-01-A — the point-buy screen at new game ✅ ALREADY SHIPPED (verified 2026-07-23).** The one-time allocation UI at `storyNewGame()` exists and is wired (see the correction above): the 27-point-buy "⚔ Choose Your Origin" modal writes `S_story.abilityScores`, guarded to fresh new game only (`_showCharCreate` clears autosave via `storyNewGame`'s `_S_DEFAULTS()` reset, respecting §STATE-INIT's single-source shape). **No design call was needed** — point-buy is the live system. Locked by `char-creation.test.js` 4/4. Not committed (user rule).
- [ ] **§CHAR-01-B (optional) — a standard-array preset origin.** Add a third origin button seeding `_cc_scores = {15,14,13,12,10,8}` (the classic array) as a one-click convenience next to custom/hard. Pure additive UI, same `storyNewGame` path, no rebalance. Low priority — the point-buy already covers this allocation manually.

**Invariants:** writes an **existing** `_S_DEFAULTS()` field (no new persistent field); DC curve unchanged (fixed point total); Free-Movement/parity N/A; no rolls (RNG N/A). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline-JS). **Verify:** `src/tests/integration/point-buy.test.js` (new game writes a valid distribution; total matches the fixed budget; a mid-game save is untouched) + `check:walk`. Sync `mechanics.md` §Character + `index.md` State Fields. **Ask-gate:** the array-vs-point-buy choice is a user design call (Loop-vs-Ask).

#### §MESH-01-HIRE — Give the hireling a voice (the §POT-C4 open slice) 🟢

**The finding (measured).** No party — and none wanted (`DUEL:CORE`/`S_story`/XP are single-combatant; MESH multiplayer is the better answer and ships). But the **§MESH-01g hired-guide bot** (`#hireling-section`, `hireling-section@2862`+) is *"a single-player companion, daily fee"* with **no `NPC_DIALOGUES` entry** — the one "companion" in the game is a daily charge with no voice.

**The design.** Give the hireling a dialogue key so it becomes a character, not a fee. Tiny, self-contained. Optionally surface a line when hired/paid.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§MESH-01-HIRE-A — a dialogue key for the guide bot.** Add an `NPC_DIALOGUES` entry (with `meta`) for the hireling and render a line at hire/daily-fee time.

**Invariants:** data + a small render hook — Free-Movement/parity/RNG all N/A. ⚠️ Hazard #1 if the render hook is inline JS. **Verify:** eyeball the hire flow (§7½) + a smoke assertion that the line renders. Sync `story.md`/`docs/story/story-arc-npc-dialogues.md`.

#### §RIPPLE-01 — Give the favor & mission-bit tracks consumers (merges §POT-P3 + §POT-W3) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Two near-dead relationship tracks have no *consequences*: **(P3)** `mission_bit` is the second-most-authored opcode — **2,448 receipts** (`_flagToLabel` rewrite `function _flagToLabel@26082`+, `token.day` timeline, `_takeMissionBit` gate-safety all shipped) — but they are read **only by gates** (i.e. only to decide whether a *later mission lists*); nothing ever *reacts* to a token you carry. **(W3)** rest is **identical at all 38 `sleep:true` nodes** and costs nothing; the file has a full favor model that a haven could hang on, but a haven's quality never tracks its resident NPC's favor.

**The design (now unblocked by §NPC-01-B).** §NPC-01-B took the NPC card from ~20 → ~203 reachable NPCs — so **NPCs now have a render surface to react *on***. P3: render a reaction line on an NPC's card when you carry a relevant mission-bit token (a receipt they'd recognize). W3: let a `sleep:true` haven's quality (rest message / a small bonus) track the resident NPC's favor — legal because **resting is not movement** (no quest/flag refuses a step). Both give the 16-bit favor track + the 2,448-token ledger their first *reactive* consumer.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-A — NPC reacts to a carried token (P3).** On the NPC card, when the player holds a mission-bit the NPC is keyed to, show a recognition line. Render-only; reuses §NPC-01's card. **Depends on §NPC-01-B (shipped).**
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-B — haven quality tracks resident favor (W3).** The rest message (and optionally a tiny rest bonus) at a `sleep:true` node varies with the resident NPC's favor. **Trap:** resting only — never touch the mover.
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-C (design call) — widen the favor track.** With ~203 card-bearing NPCs, the 16-`favor`-bit track is too thin to earn the ladder; overlaps **§NPC-01-D**. Coordinate — do not double-scope.

**Invariants:** render + rest only — **Free-Movement untouched** (resting is not a step); outside all parity fences; RNG N/A (or seeded for any bonus roll). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline JS). **Verify:** `src/tests/integration/npc-token-reaction.test.js` (card shows the line only when the token is held) + a rest-quality assertion + eyeball (§7½). Sync `story.md`/`docs/story/story-arc-npc-dialogues.md`. **Coordinate with §NPC-01-D** on the favor-widening.

#### §MUD-01 — A text/MUD-style view over the shipped room engine (the §POT-S3 unblocked half) 🟡

**The finding (measured).** `ROOMS:CORE`'s `describeCell` (`function describeCell@10150`; kernel `ROOMS:CORE:START@9985`) already produces full room prose + per-direction exits with `__roadDestination` hints — but its full output is exercised mainly by `src/tests/mud-harness.mjs` (**269/269**); the player sees only node text + the §NAV-01-FU road-hover tooltip. *"Describe a vivid environment"* is close to what `describeCell` **already returns.**

**The design.** A text/MUD-style view is **mostly a renderer over shipped output** — surface `describeCell`'s prose + exits as an in-game panel. The *"I declare how my character interacts"* half is **§POT-R1 (BLOCKED on §VM-01 Inc A)** and is explicitly **out of scope** — this track ships only the **read** half (the view), which is unblocked and cheap.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§MUD-01-A — the descriptive view.** Render `describeCell(world, pos)`'s prose + exit list as a read-only in-game panel (reuse the harness's proven output). No input parser (that's §POT-R1).

**Invariants:** ⚠️ **`ROOMS:CORE` (`ROOMS:CORE:START@9985`) is parity-fenced** — read its output, never edit the inlined copy; if `src/js/rooms.js` is touched, re-inline + `src/scripts/check-rooms-parity.js`. Render-only; Free-Movement N/A. **Verify:** reuse `src/tests/mud-harness.mjs` expectations for the rendered strings + eyeball (§7½). Sync `maps.md`/`docs/spec/spec-corridors.md`.

#### §HINT-01 — State-aware hints (needs a design call first) 🟡

**The finding (measured).** Quests carry a `hint:` field, **widely authored** (`grep -c "hint:"` = **1,057**), stating the stat + DC outright (e.g. *"See through the mockery — WIS Insight DC 11"*). Today's hint is a **constant string** — it doesn't know your inventory, level, or favor.

**The tension (why this needs a design call, not a loop task).** §POT-M2 wants a hint that knows *your* state — but this collides head-on with §PLAY-01's **honesty theme**: a hint that *hides* the DC to seem cleverer would be **less** honest, not more. The value depends entirely on which way it goes. **Do not build before the design call is made.**

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§HINT-01-0 — the design call (ASK).** Decide the shape: does a state-aware hint *add* context ("you already hold the Van Helsing route map") while **keeping** the DC visible (honesty-preserving), or does it replace the static string? Present the options; do not default. Only then scope A.

**Invariants:** TBD by the design call; if built, render-only. **Verify:** TBD. **This is an Ask-gate, not a loop item.**

#### §INNER-01 — A mechanic for the inner arc (the §POT-X2 design track; lab report first) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Yael's monologue promises an inner arc outright — *"You came here to fight demons… **the ones behind your own eyes are older and meaner, and that is the real fight."*** There is **no mechanic for that fight**. The one shipped inner-arc mechanic is §GR-D's **Froberger Entry 42** (`entry42Written`/`entry42Text`/`entry42Read` (`entry42Written: false, entry42Text@23125`); `quest_ng_02` "The Open Page" (`quest_ng_02@11048`); carried across NG+) — the blank page you may fill at ≥3 dear friends, at the very end of the game. Entry 42 is **the proof the shape works and the template for more.**

**The design (a design call + lab report, NOT a loop task).** Extending the inner arc is a **new narrative arc** → per the Lab Report Policy it needs `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-inner-arc.md` **locking data shapes before any HTML edit**, and it needs a **user design call** on what the mechanic *is* (a reflective journal like Entry 42, a fear/resolve track, moral weight — several are possible and they are not interchangeable). This row is a **placeholder for that design conversation**, not buildable inline.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§INNER-01-0 — design call + lab report.** Decide the mechanic's shape (with the user); lock data shapes in a lab report; only then scope increments. Reuse Entry 42's cross-NG+ persistence pattern as the template.

**Invariants:** TBD by the lab report; likely render + a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field (declare once, per §STATE-INIT). **Verify:** TBD. **Design-call + lab-report gated.**

### §VM-01-B-FU — Should "a save fully determines the future" include the fight? (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02cn, 🟡 DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§VM-01-B-FU — §VM-01-B seeded the four pipelines the server owns and deliberately fenced combat out; thirty days on the fence is still exactly where it was, and nothing has ever filed the question it deferred.** The seeded stream reaches encounter fire (`if (_seededNext() < baseRate)@28442`), the skill-check d20 (`const d20  = Math.ceil(E.rng() * 20)@22249`), the loot/drop pipeline and the monster draw. **It does not reach the fight**: story-battle d20s, damage dice, death saves and flee rolls are among the **49 `Math.random()` code sites left at HEAD**, alongside `function roll(sides)@6417` and `function rollN(sides, count)@6421`. **Why it matters now and did not then:** §VM-01-E's soft-lock prover consumes the seam — `src/tests/integration/uqf-softlock.test.js` asserts *"the same rngState → byte-identical outcome across two runs"* — so replay-from-save is a **working diagnostic**, and it goes blind the moment a battle starts. A player who loses a run inside combat still cannot hand anyone a reproducible artifact. **The design call is genuinely open, and §VM-01-B argued both sides:** determinism helps *diagnosis* and may flatten the *swing of a fight* (reload-scum a death save and you get the same death save). **Three options:** (a) leave it — combat entropy is a feature, the fence stays a fence; (b) seed combat behind an off-by-default debug flag, so a bug report can be replayed without changing the shipped feel; (c) seed it outright and accept that a reloaded battle repeats. **Not scoped as code until the call is made.** Note the MP duel path is already seeded independently (`function __duelRng(seed)@10304`) and is out of scope either way. **Related, not duplicate:** §AUDIT-03bg is about the *rate* the two sides compose around a stream that is already byte-identical; this is about which rolls draw from the stream at all.

### §VM-01-G4c-FU — The nine D1 surfaces the shipped grammar cannot express (NEW 2026-08-04, 🟡 THREE ASKS)

- [ ] **§VM-01-G4c-FU — three design calls, each blocking a group of D1 verbs.** **Measured at ship time of §VM-01-G4c**, which migrated the 4 of 13 D1 surfaces the grammar already expresses. The other 9 are not "the rest of the list" — each is blocked by a shape the VM does not have, and inventing one to satisfy two or three sites is the `itemsMinAny` failure the whole track exists to prevent. **(1) The delayed beat — Harbor Board (`STN`) · Solvak probe (`VS`) · Seal delivery (`VS`).** Each ends `setTimeout(() => storyMsg(…), 400/600/800)`. **`storyMsg` REPLACES `#story-move-msg`**, so the first line is *destroyed* after the beat — that pacing is authored, not incidental. `narrative` has no timing field and the kernel is pure (it cannot schedule), so folding both lines into the driver's buffer would make a transient line permanent. **ASK:** accept the merge (both lines survive, joined — arguably an improvement, and it is what §VM-01-G4c's migration already does *elsewhere*: Yva's speech and the firewood narrative used to be overwritten and now survive), or give the **host** driver a `delayMs` on `narrative` and keep the beat? **Recommendation: accept the merge**, because it is the same thing the slice already did at two other sites and it loses no words. **(2) Panel chrome — Surge Lock (`NUE`, the only `cost` `resource` site in the game) · Froberger sealed letter (`TLS`) · Codex Inquisitor (`NUE`).** Each hand-builds a `.sweelinck-variant` div with its own `border-left-color`, title colour, wrapper `margin-top` (8 vs 10), title `margin-bottom` (4 vs 6), body `margin-bottom` (6 vs 8) and, at Surge Lock, a button `background`. A **byte-identical** generic panel would need all six as registry fields — a CSS struct smuggled into a vocabulary. **ASK:** normalize the three to one panel look (a deliberate, small visual change, and then `panel:{title, body, accent}` is a real shape), or leave them inline as hand-styled one-offs? **Recommendation: normalize** — three sites is enough to make a shape, and the alternative leaves the only `resource` price in the game un-migrated. **(3) A ceremonia launch — Codex Inquisitor (also).** Its button calls `_rollCeremonia(questId)`: it *starts* a skill check rather than containing one, which is exactly why the `skill_check` scope fence does not need widening — but there is no bit that starts one. **ASK:** add a `ceremonia`/`roll_quest` bit, or leave the launcher inline? **Recommendation: leave it** until a second consumer exists (the §5 free-text rule: grow the shape at three, not two). **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-23 (§DOC-02dc): the second-consumer bar is ALREADY MET, and the ask states two different bars in one sentence.** `_rollCeremonia` is called from **two** button handlers inside G4's own region — the Codex Inquisitor (l. 35067) **and the Memory Gate** (l. 34980, `mg-attempt-btn` → `_rollCeremonia('quest_d0204_a1')`), the latter censused by the report itself in §2 and again in its three-currency table. A third launcher exists outside the region at the quest-list roll button (l. 35649), which is not a verb surface. So: *"until a second consumer"* is satisfied; *"three, not two"* is satisfied only if the quest-list button counts. **Re-state the ask against two verb-surface consumers, not one**, and note that the Memory Gate is also blocked by §DX-02fv. **Also recorded, not a blocker:** at `NUE` the verb driver's completion re-render advances `npcVisitCounts['sq_revisit']`, because that block counts a **render, not an arrival** — so Sweelinck's once-per-run age line now lands immediately after the S49 click instead of on the next visit. It still fires exactly once, and the per-render counter is pre-existing (any `storyRender` re-entry at NUE already ticks it). Fixing it is a content-timing decision, not a migration step. **Not in this row:** the Reading Circle (a day cooldown — belongs to quests' `retryable`/`retryGateDays`, lab report §9.3) and the la_riva delivery (it lives inside CDG's `cq-boss-buttons` container, so it ships with **G4d**).

### §VM-01-G-FU-f2 — The three engine specials (NEW 2026-08-05, ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-06)

- [x] **§VM-01-G-FU-f2 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-06 `9c9fe42` — all three asks ANSWERED by the user 2026-08-06, each per the filed recommendation: (1) TLS → node field · (2) INN pricing STAYS · (3) patrol ordered-visit STAYS.** The one code slice: TLS authors **`finalBattle:{minLevel:20,minShards:7}`** in `NODE_MAP` (written through `WBAPI.editStructuredField` in-process, server stopped — 1-line data diff) and the new single helper **`_finalBattleReady(code)`** replaces the three hand-copied `=== 'TLS' && level>=20 && shards>=7` predicates at the encounter card + both quest-list Fight buttons — data + one reader, the §VM-01-G3 `onActivate` precedent, no grammar growth; a node without the field is never final. Decisions (2) and (3) are DECIDED-STAY, not open: the Innmother's waive stays one-inn until a second inn wants it (re-open only then, as a new row), and the patrol ladder stays an engine special serving its one quest (the §5 rule). **Evidence:** `vm01f2-finalbattle-field.test.js` **5/5 with 3 red on HEAD** (exactly the source/data/helper migration tests) and the 2 behaviour pins green BOTH ways (below-threshold plain Fight · at-20/7 THE FINAL BATTLE — Commander Bruhns + 🔥 FINAL FIGHT + mq_7's 🔥 Final Fight) — the honest no-op shape; §7½ eyeballed live both sides of the threshold; `check:walk` 16/16 (the behaviour gate printed ✓ then hung at 0% CPU — the documented detach artifact, 3rd sighting; all gates re-run individually exit 0); anchors:fix 112 hints in-slice; mechanics.md's Final Boss row re-anchored to live `TLS` (was legacy `CO`; `shards = 7` corrected to `≥`); full Playwright **935 passed / 4 failed (940)** — the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` set, `multiplayer-presence:171` flaked and retried green. **The below-front stack and the §11 inventory are now FULLY CLOSED — blocks and specials both.** Original row (the measurement + the three asks as filed): **Measured live at §VM-01-G-FU-f ship time** (the census's "genuine engine specials" row; the SSJ block that shipped beside them was the last *block* — these are not blocks but predicates woven into engine paths). **(1) TLS `isFinal` framing — 3 sites, and the duplication is the risk.** The predicate `(node|waypointNode) === 'TLS' && level >= 20 && shards >= 7` swaps `storyPreBattle` for `storyPreFinalBattle` and relabels the button 🔥 FINAL FIGHT, hand-copied at the encounter card and BOTH quest-list Fight buttons — three copies of one rule that must never disagree. **ASK:** a node field (`finalBattle:{ minLevel, minShards }` on TLS, read by one helper) vs stay engine-special? A node field is §VM-01-G3's `onActivate` precedent (per-node data driving an engine seam), not grammar growth. **(2) INN sleep pricing — 3 sites.** `freeBookingUnlocked` (written once at `innmotherKindness >= 5`, and `quest_inn_06`'s completion flag) zeroes `node.sleepCost` in the sleep flow, every site guarded `node.code === 'INN'` — the Innmother's kindness reward is invisible to every other inn in the game by construction. **ASK:** generalize to a node/data shape (e.g. a `sleepCostWaiveFlag` node field any inn could author) vs stay a one-inn special? **(3) Patrol ordered-visit — 3 arrival-path lines.** While `quest_city_watch_patrol` is active, arrivals write `patrolBA → patrolIN → patrolRouteComplete` in enforced LHR→TLL→MHQ order; the quest completes on the last flag. The gate grammar has no ordered-visit leaf, and this is its only consumer — the §5 free-text rule says don't grow a shape for one site. **ASK:** stay (an engine special serving one quest) vs re-express as a 3-quest chain (each leg `atNode`-completed, `unlock`-chained — no grammar growth, but three quest rows where the fiction has one) vs a `visitedSeq` gate leaf (grammar growth at one consumer)? **Recommendation: (1) node field, (2) stay until a second inn wants it, (3) stay** — but all three are the user's calls, filed not defaulted (the G-FU-d GCI precedent).

### §DX-02m — Unseeded `Math.random()` in state-writing paths (NEW 2026-08-04, 🟠)

- [ ] **§DX-02m — invariant #6 has no gate, and at least one path writes persisted state off the unseeded stream.** **Found by §VM-01-G2b's golden diff**, which is the honest way it surfaced: 16 of 17 combos were byte-identical across the migration and the 17th (`HKG`) differed **between two runs of the same code**. Cause: the CY Madness Gate (`_nodeHookBirkaCyMadnessGate`) rolls its WIS save with `Math.random()` and picks its madness line with `Math.random()`, then writes **`S_story.cyMadnessRoll`** and **`S_story.cyMadnessTable`** — persisted save fields. prompt.md §6 invariant 6 says game-state randomness draws the seeded stream (`_seededNext()`), so a save no longer determines this outcome. **Measured scope: 51 `Math.random()` call sites** in `play.html`; how many reach persisted state is the row's first job (the CY gate is one, found by accident). **Nothing gates this today** — `check:rng` (gate #10) proves the three mulberry32 streams agree with each other, not *which* stream a state write draws from. **Suggested shape** (the §DX-02g/§AUDIT-03j house style): a scanner that classifies every `Math.random()` site as **COSMETIC** (allowed, with a reason) or **STATE** (must use the seeded stream), fail on an unclassified one — a percentage heuristic will not do, because most of the 51 legitimately are cosmetic. **Trap recorded by §DX-02f:** a test that stubs an RNG must stub the stream the code actually draws; converting a site changes which stream it reads, so any test pinning that outcome moves with it. **+11 named sites, the largest single-feature cluster yet (§DOC-02m, 2026-08-12):** the whole Yugurt Lake fishing path — eight in `` `function storyFishing() {@30393` `` (the DEX cast die, both Catch dice, the Type die, the species pick, the night-pool pick, the bait pick and the bait-search die) and three in the kill/lake-magic path around `` `function _lakeMagicBonuses() {@23421` ``'s consumers — every one feeding persisted state (`S_story.fishingCatchLog`, `S_story.inventory`, and through the battle `S_story.hp`). Same shape as §DOC-02l's epic negotiation rolls and §DOC-02k's Codex Core rolls: **the UQF quest path is seeded and every hand-authored surface that rolls its own dice is not**, because §VM-01-B moved the d20 inside `_resolveQuestUQF` and never reached the surfaces outside it. (Currently masked by §FISH-01 — the fishing path cannot be entered at all — so fix §FISH-01 first or the conversion is untestable.) **§DOC-02n adds a named instance OUTSIDE any fishing/quest path and the highest-stakes one yet:** `Math.ceil(Math.random() * 20) + _luckMod()@25897` rolls **story death saves** off the unseeded stream, as does the Indomitable reroll three lines below — a life-or-death outcome mutating persisted HP, so a seeded replay cannot reproduce a run that ended in death. **§DOC-02aa +2, and they are the first sites on the NEW-GAME path:** `S_story.frobergerNoteNode = _ebPool[Math.floor(Math.random() * _ebPool.length)];@23981` and its verbatim twin inside `function storyNewGamePlus() {@24002` pick, from the unseeded stream, **which of 20 Epic Battlegrounds hides Froberger's last note** — then persist it. So the same seed does not produce the same world on either `storyNewGame` or NG+, and the choice is made before the player has rolled anything. Cheap to convert (`_seededNext()`), and it belongs in the conversion's first batch because it is the earliest state write in a run.

### §DX-02y — `S_story.acBonus` is a READ-ONLY field: one reader, zero writers, born dead (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02t, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02y — the levelling AC bonus has never accrued, and the home doc says it does.** `S_story.acBonus` is declared twice (`acBonus: 0,@23041` in `_S_DEFAULTS()`, and again in the `S_story` seed literal at `level: 1, atkBonus: 0, acBonus: 0,@23075`), read **exactly once** — the `(S_story.acBonus || 0)` term inside `function _calcPlayerAc() {@24611` — and **written nowhere in 38,712 lines**. `git log -S` returns **0 commits** for any assignment to it, and the field is equally writer-less at the earliest surviving build `32c10c5`: **born dead, not retired.** Its origin document (`docs/lab-reports/lab-report-leveling-flashbang-condition-economy.md`, Layer 18) specifies +1 AC at L5, +1 at L8 and +2 at L10; none of it exists, so a level-20 character's AC is base + shield + the §DROP-03 lake term and nothing else.
> **This is the INVERSE of the §DX-02n class, and the reason `check:deadconsts` must census readers and writers SEPARATELY.** §DX-02n's members are write-only — dead weight, harmless. This one is **read-only: a live consumer with a broken dependency**, silently contributing 0 to a number the player sees. A gate scoped to *unread* fields walks straight past it. Same argument §DX-02u already makes from the other direction; this is its second axis and the two should ship as one phase (fail on **either** count being zero).
> **Doc-side defect FIXED in this increment (two-way sync):** `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md` stated *"`atkBonus` and `acBonus` in `S_story` are updated immediately"* at level-up. Neither is true. Corrected in place with an annotated note — the §DOC-02j `LOOT_TABLE` rot direction, and the more dangerous one, because a reader greps the doc and concludes the field works.
> **Fix — two options, and the choice is small but real.** (a) **Delete the field** and its `_calcPlayerAc` term: three lines, and honest. (b) **Wire it**, either to the `FIGHTER_FEATURES` track or to a DEX-derived AC. **Prefer (a) unless a design call wants levelled AC** — nothing in the 79-day-old shipped design grants AC by level, and an unwired field that renders as 0 is exactly the shape that produced this row.

### §AUDIT-03ae — the attack roll adds DEX *and* STR, and the character sheet double-counts STR against itself (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02t, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03ae — three surfaces disagree about what a story attack roll is, and every identifier involved resolves.** `S_story.atkBonus` was specified (Layer 18) as a *levelling* bonus. At HEAD it carries the **STR modifier**: seeded at character creation by `S_story.atkBonus = Math.max(0, Math.floor((scores.str - 10) / 2));@23959` and raised only by an ASI STR bump (`if (strDelta > 0) S_story.atkBonus@38522`). The Layer-18 integration point survives unchanged as `const lvlAtk@25031`. Two individually-correct changes compose into a defect:
> **(1) Combat adds both abilities.** `_overlayPlayerAttack` takes its ability modifier from `getAtkAbilityMod()`, which reads the dice-roller's own control — and that control is `<option value="dex" selected>@3719`. The story→simulator sync at `document.getElementById('char-level').value = _slv;@24676` writes level, AC, max HP and all six ability scores but **never repoints the select**, so every story swing rolls **d20 + DEX mod + prof + STR mod** (+ weapon/tome/lake/ally).
> **(2) The character sheet double-counts STR.** `const atkTotal = strMod + profBonus + (S_story.atkBonus || 0);@37599` — and `atkBonus` *is* `strMod`.
> **(3) The sheet contradicts its own breakdown.** It renders the bonus term as `atkBonus − strMod`, i.e. `+0`, so the printed components sum to `strMod + prof` while the printed total is `2 × strMod + prof`. Worked example (STR 16, DEX 12, prof +2): the sheet prints **"+8 (STR+3 + Prof+2 + bonus+0)"** — a total its own parts sum to 5 — while combat actually rolls at **+6**.
> **No symbol census finds this**: every identifier resolves, every line is live, and no gate compares two readers' *interpretations* of one field. It is visible only by asking what the field MEANS at each reader — the same shape as §AUDIT-03m's "worse than dead" codes, in arithmetic rather than in identifiers.
> **Fix — decide the contract first, then make all three agree.** A Fighter Champion should key attack on STR: point `atk-ability` at `str` in the story sync **and** drop `atkBonus` from `lvlAtk` (or from `atkTotal`), so STR is counted exactly once in both places. **Pin it with a test that asserts the character sheet's printed total equals the engine's rolled bonus** — the property, not the incident. Interacts with **§DX-02y**: both rows are the Layer-18 stat fields drifting away from their single reader, and they are cheapest fixed together.

### §DX-02z — nothing pairs `CONDITION_ITEMS` with `CONDITION_GOLD`, and the fallback is the retired price scale (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02t, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02z — a 13th condition item ships silently at 20 gp, the pre-Layer-18 price.** All five cost lookups read `CONDITION_GOLD[…] || 20`. Today the two tables are a clean bijection — every one of the 12 `const CONDITION_ITEMS = [@22410` `match` names has a row in `const CONDITION_GOLD = {@24619` — so the fallback is unreachable and this is **latent, not live**. But the fallback value *is* the scale §DOC-02t's origin report multiplied away: adding a condition item without a price row re-creates the exact defect the ×100 repricing was written to fix (a 3-round ADV-plus-status effect costing less than a healing potion), and it does it **silently**, since a missing key and a 20 gp key are indistinguishable at the call site.
> **Two independent reasons this is worth a gate rather than a comment.** (a) The two tables are **~2,200 lines apart** and neither names the other. (b) Conditions are **not inventory-gated** — `// All condition items available for gold purchase (not inventory gated)@36385` — so **gold is the only brake** on the strongest pre-battle effect in the game, and the price table is carrying the entire balance load alone.
> **Fix:** a `check:dupkeys`- or `check:deadconsts`-adjacent phase asserting the two key sets are equal in both directions (an unpriced item **and** an orphaned price both fail), plus dropping the `|| 20` fallbacks so a miss throws instead of mispricing. Also sweep the stale comment `// filtered CONDITION_ITEMS matching current inventory@36377`, contradicted by the engine's own comment 8 lines below it. **Deferred from this increment because it is an HTML edit** and the working tree carries an uncommitted user change to `play.html`.

### §AUDIT-03ad — the §WISDOM-01 arc advertises a DC 14 that is not a roll (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02r, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03ad — `W6 here (WIS 14 or combat)@33494` names a check the engine has never made.** `quest_wis_06` ships `type:'side'` with `bits:[]`; both W6 paths are `storyRender` buttons. `Accept the reflection (receive the shadow@33471` grants `wisPage6_shadow`, a Shadow Shard and **+350 XP unconditionally** — no `skill_check` bit, no `dc:`, no d20 anywhere in the block — and the fight branch (`label:'Shadow — The Mirror Construct'@33488`, `key:'shadow'` ac 12) sets the same flag. Roen's portfolio line enumerates all six fragments, and its **other five entries are exact against the shipped bits** (W1 WIS 13 · W2 WIS 12 · W3 INT 11 · W4 INT 12 · W5 WIS 12). Both facts are present at the arc's birth commit `e339aeb`: **born broken, 76 days live.**
> **This is the §AUDIT-03v/w cluster's sharpest instance: the string is correct five times before it lies once, which is exactly what makes it credible.** The previous members of that cluster promise a mechanic that does not exist anywhere, so a grep finds nothing and the reader knows.
> **Two fixes, both small.** Either add `{ kind:'skill_check', stat:'WIS', skill:'Insight', dc:14, onPass:[…], onFail:[…] }` and move the accept effects onto `onPass` (which is what the design says), or edit two words in the portfolio string. **Prefer the first** — the §DOC-02r measurement is that W6 is the arc's rhetorical keystone (the "hardest to see is yourself" gradient) and it is currently its only free branch.
> **Detector wanted (mechanical, no judgement): a DC stated in a player-facing string with no matching `dc:` in the quest that string names.** Three of the four §AUDIT-03v/w/y instances filed since 2026-08-11 would have been caught by it. **Blocked in practice by §AUDIT-03x** — `quest_wis_06` sits on non-primary `VS`, so the fix is untestable in-game until the cell-primacy row lands.
> **⟶ SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME BLOCK, found 2026-08-13 (§DOC-02as) — fix both in one edit. 🟡 one small call.** `quest_wis_06`'s activation gate is `` `activateNode:'VS', gate:{ flags:['visbyUnderground'] },@13479` ``, so it lists **the moment the player descends** — but the accept/fight buttons it points at live in `` `} else if (S_story.wisHookReceived && _allFive && !_p6) {@33467` ``, i.e. they render **only after the other five fragments are complete**. Until then the panel shows a fragment tally and no buttons, while an active quest whose `hint` reads *"Use the story panel at VS to choose: accept or fight"* sits in the log pointing at it. **This falsifies the arc's own headline design thesis** — the §WISDOM-01 report's §V calls it *"the first arc with parallel fragment collection … the player can do fragments in any order because each law is independent"* — for exactly one of six: W1–W5 are genuinely parallel, W6 is silently last. **The call:** either make the shadow choice render at `_p6 === false` regardless of the other five (matching the quest's own gate and the design), or gate `quest_wis_06` on the five page flags so the log stops promising a surface that is not there. **Prefer the first** — W6 is the arc's rhetorical keystone and the report's *"the hardest person to read is yourself"* gradient only lands if it can be reached out of order like the rest. Note the two defects share one block, so the `skill_check` retrofit this row already asks for should land in the same edit.

### §DX-02cn — a New Game inherits the previous character's economy identity, because the ledger's bearer credential is not in `_S_DEFAULTS()` (NEW 2026-08-17 during §DOC-02bv, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02cn — `S_story.playerKey` and `S_story.pvpOff` are written into the save and declared nowhere, and `storyNewGame` cannot clear a field it has never heard of.** 🟢 **two declarations, ~10 minutes.** §STATE-INIT made `` `const _S_DEFAULTS = () => ({@23063` `` the single source of truth for a fresh `S_story` shape, precisely because the drifted seed literal above it had left `visitedCells`/`playerR`/`playerC` undefined and crashed `cellMove` on the first move. **Two mesh fields skipped that contract.** §MESH-01i slice 2b introduced `` `function _mpPlayerKey()@28981` `` — 32 hex from `crypto.getRandomValues`, minted once, and, in the function's own comment, *"kept in the save file like any other `S_story` field"* — because the server derives the durable economy identity from it: `player8 = sha256(playerKey).slice(0,8)` (`src/js/wbapi-server.js:function playerKeyRegister@290`), which keys the hash-chained item ledger at `ledger/<serverId>.jsonl`. `S_story.pvpOff` (read at `storyRender`, written by the PvP toggle) skipped it too. **Neither appears anywhere in the `_S_DEFAULTS()` body**, verified by grep over the whole literal. **The consequence is not the usual undefined-field crash — it is the opposite, and it is worse.** `storyNewGame` resets by `Object.assign(S_story, _S_DEFAULTS())` **on the live object**, not by replacing the binding, so a field absent from the defaults shape is not overwritten and **survives a New Game**. A player who starts a fresh character therefore keeps the previous character's `playerKey`, resolves to the same `ledgerPid`, and **inherits the old character's item-ownership chain** — the exact provenance guarantee the no-dupe ledger exists to provide. `_mpPlayerKey()`'s regex test self-heals a *missing* key but has nothing to say about a *stale* one. NG+ (`storyNgPlus`) is arguably the case where carrying the key forward is correct, which is why this wants a declaration rather than a blanket wipe. **Fix:** declare `playerKey: ''` and `pvpOff: false` in `_S_DEFAULTS()`; NG+ already re-applies the fields it means to preserve through its explicit `saved*` locals, so add `playerKey` there if the same player should keep their chain across a NG+ run (design-neutral — both readings are defensible and the row does not need the call to land the declaration). **Related:** the report that surfaced this stated the invariant as *"MP state … never in the save file"* — the narrow form, *"no session id in the save"*, is still true today. **Risk:** low; `pvpOff: false` matches the current `!!undefined` behaviour exactly and `playerKey: ''` matches the mint-on-miss test. **Verify:** a spec that mints a key, calls `storyNewGame`, and asserts `S_story.playerKey` is empty (or deliberately preserved, per the call); plus `_S_DEFAULTS()` gains two keys and the fresh-load regression test stays green.

### §AUDIT-03bl — the §KG corridor's XP model is 199 short of the Level-6 it promises, and the report's own contingency lever does not close it (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cb, 🟡 one design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bl — the band advertises L1→~L6 and the minimum path lands at Level 5.** 🟡 **arithmetic is settled; the call is which lever to pull.** `0, 400, 1000, 2000, 3500, 5500, 8000, 11000, 15000, 20000,@24420` puts L6 at **5,500**. Kill XP is `const xpAward = Math.round((S.enemy.ac || 10) * (S.opp.maxHp || 10)@25293` × `partyMult` (1.0 solo), so the lab report's `AC·maxHP` metric is correct and all six per-monster products are exact as tabled (45 · 96 · 196 · 130 · 208 · 390). Three derived figures are not. **Quest-completion XP is 2,790, not the 2,990 claimed** — an addition error over eleven values the report itself lists. **Battle XP on the minimum path is 2,511, not 2,707** — the report assumed `gladiator_bot ×4` (kg_05's 1 + kg_06's 3), but the counter is cumulative and never resets, so kg_05's kill *counts toward* kg_06's `≥3` and the true minimum is ×3. **Total 5,301 — 199 short.** Chain gold is **450**, not the ≈550 stated.
> **The report anticipated this and budgeted the fix against the wrong total.** Its stated lever — *"the two capstone rewards (kg_09 400→450, kg_11 500→600)"* — is **+150**, reaching 5,451. **Still 49 short.** The contingency could not have worked even if a smoke test had triggered it.
> **THE CALL.** (a) **Retune** — raise the two capstones far enough to clear 5,500 on the minimum path, keeping the cull quotas as authored so pacing rather than arithmetic drives the level-up (the report's own stated principle). (b) **Amend the promise** — §XP-01 added effort XP for misses and failed checks (`const EFFORT_XP_PCT = 0.25;@24427`, 2026-07-12) and §XP-02-A added flat first-arrival exploration XP, neither available on 2026-07-08; a real player clears L6 on those grants plus incidental encounters, so the band may simply be re-documented as L1→~L6-with-exploration. **(a) is the honest one** — the pacing currently survives by accident. Either way this is moot until **§DX-02cy** lands, because no §KG cull quest can complete at all today.
> **Also correct `plan-archive.md` §KG Increment 3**, whose ship record repeats the report's figures verbatim (*"≈2,990 quest-XP + ≈2,700 quota-battle-XP on the minimum path"*).

### §AUDIT-03bm — *all action earns XP* reaches 3.5 % of skill checks, because 92.6 % of them pay in tokens rather than XP on the PASS side too (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cd, 🟡 design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bp — the tenth name on a 2026-07-23 side-finding list is the one the fix did not reach, and it has rendered on no node since (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02cl).** 🟢 **one short profile entry OR one curated-list addition; no design call.** `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-npc-card-map.md` §7 SF2 named **ten** dialogue-only NPCs with no `BIRKA_NPC_PROFILES` entry; `ed06625` shipped the fix and its own comment correctly says **nine**. The tenth is `don_fluffissimo` — *Don Fluffissimo, Fat Merchant Cat boss (antagonist)*, `NPC_DIALOGUES` entry with a full `meta` block declaring `node:"CDG"` — and it was never in `birkaNpcs` to begin with, so the SF2 relaxation of the `!p` guard could not reach it: that fix only helps a key something already asks the renderer to draw. **Measured in a browser at HEAD:** the union of the curated literal and `function _deriveNpcRenderMap@23673` spans **125 of 416 nodes** and **204 keys**, of which 203 render; `don_fluffissimo` is in neither set, because the derived map inverts `BIRKA_NPC_PROFILES` and it has no profile, while the curated `CDG` entry is `_cqNpcs` = jimmy / sandy_cat / kenickie. **It is the third face of one registry-mismatch class:** §NPC-01-SF2 closed *dialogue without profile, but mapped*; §AUDIT-03bo is open on *profile without dialogue*; this row is *dialogue without profile and unmapped* — the only one of the three that no gate and no test can currently see, since both existing checks start from a key some registry already lists. **Why it matters:** CDG is the Cat Quarter, the arc's own antagonist node, and the boss of the Corrupted Cats has an authored `worldTruth` (*"Power moves slowly, deliberately, and with bodyguards"*) that no player can reach. **Fix (pick one):** add a `BIRKA_NPC_PROFILES.don_fluffissimo` entry with `node:"CDG"` so the derived map picks it up — or, if the boss should appear only once the arc has escalated, append it to `_cqNpcs` behind the same quest gate `sandy_cat` and `kenickie` already use. The second is the honest reading of a boss. **Verify:** extend the §NPC-01-SF2 block in `src/tests/integration/npc-card-map.test.js` to assert that **every** `NPC_DIALOGUES` key carrying a `meta.node` that resolves in `NODE_MAP` is reachable from either the curated literal or the derived map — the general assertion that would have caught this in 2026-07-23, and would close all three faces at once.

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bo — one NPC has a name, an occupation, a home node and two quests, and has never rendered a single pixel (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02ck).** 🟢 **one short dialogue entry; no design call beyond writing four lines of voice.** `watcher_gvw: { key@22993` is **The Greenwood Watcher**, *natural historian of cognition, forest warden*, `node:"GVW"` — a full `BIRKA_NPC_PROFILES` entry, present in the derived render map, and the declared quest-giver for `clr_01_act3@21942` and `clr_01_act4` of the five-part *I Just Know This* skill-check chain. It has **no `NPC_DIALOGUES` entry**, and `function _renderNpcCard@23684` resolves the dialogue before it does anything else, so the card returns empty. **Measured in a browser at HEAD, driving the real page:** the derived map spans 121 nodes and 204 keys; **203 of the 204 render a card and get a §NPC-01-D Talk button — this one renders nothing and gets neither.** It is the exact mirror of the case §NPC-01-A/SF2 fixed (dialogue without profile, which now renders from `dlg.meta`), and the mirror was never closed: profile without dialogue still falls off the surface silently, with no gate watching. **Why it matters beyond one card:** the two `clr_01` quests were anchored to this key by §AUDIT-03g's derivation sweep, so `./api.sh audit` reports `errors: 0` for a quest-giver the player cannot see, and the §NPC-01-C ⚔/✦ reveal, the Talk verb and the favor ladder are all unreachable for it by construction. **Fix:** add a `NPC_DIALOGUES.watcher_gvw` entry with `meta` (`name`, `occupation`, `worldTruth`, `enemy` — every one of the 213 existing entries carries a `worldTruth`, so omitting it would make this the first) plus an `impartial` pool; the profile already supplies the name and node. **Verify:** a census assertion that every `BIRKA_NPC_PROFILES` key in the derived render map resolves in `NPC_DIALOGUES` — the general gate this row wants, so the mirror case cannot silently reopen — plus a render pin at `GVW`. Add it to `src/tests/integration/npc-card-map.test.js` beside the §NPC-01-SF2 block that fixed the other direction.

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bn — the enemy-AI classifier calls three lake fish the Void and lets a Death Knight run away.** 🟡 **content/vocabulary row; no engine change, and the report that shipped it predicted this exact failure.** §PLAY-01-B classifies every enemy as Void-touched or mundane beast at ≤30 % HP: Void enemies press (a tier-scaled enrage), mundane beasts roll `` `function _fleeChance@25165` `` and escape. With the terrain half of the test inert (§DX-02di), the name regex `` `const _VOID_ENEMY_RE@25144` `` is the **entire** classifier, and it is a literary instrument doing a taxonomic job. **Census** (`src/js/wbapi-core.js` `W.load`, 398 monsters): **34 tagged Void (8.5 %)** — which is dead centre of the *"~30–40 Void-aligned entries"* the report itself estimated for the data pass it chose **not** to do, so the reach is right and only the membership is wrong. **Four false positives, three of them fish:** `fish_19` *Dread Catfish of Yugurt* (deadly) and `fish_20` *Yugurt's Dread* (deadly) match on `Dread`; `night_04` *Shadowfin Carp* matches on `Shadow`; `fiend_beast` *Fiend Beast* — the word **beast** is in its name — matches on `Fiend`. `fish_19`/`fish_20` are ranks 19–20 of the twenty-rank lake ladder, and §FC06 makes fishing the **sole** source of +1..+4 gear, so every player who wants magic weapons fights them; `` `function _startFishBattle@30628` `` routes through the pre-battle overlay straight into `` `function _storyEnemyTurn@25192` ``. **Proved live at `BOO` (terrain `yugurt_lake`)** driving the real loader: atk 12 → 16 and the battle log reads *"⚫ The Void presses in — the wound only makes it worse. (+4 atk, +4 dmg, +1d12)"* — a **catfish** announced to the player as the Void, at the hardest press in the table. **Twenty-two false negatives among unambiguous undead, four of them `deadly`:** Vampire · Mummy Lord · Death Knight · The Bruxa of Corvo Bianco (deadly); Vampire Spawn · Mummy · Banshee · Spirit Naga · Bruxa (Higher Vampire) · Grave Hag · Mummy Priest · Cyber-Vampire · Vampire (Higher) · Woodland Spirit (hard); Ghost · Hym (Curse Spirit) · Pirate Ghost · Bone Naga · Library Ghost · Graveir (medium); Zombie · Protofleder (easy). The 24-alternative regex carries `wight`, `lich`, `revenant` and `wretch` but not `zombie`, `vampire`, `mummy`, `ghost`, `banshee` or `bone` — and **Hym (Curse Spirit) misses by one letter**, because the regex asks for `cursed`. These are therefore **mundane beasts** and roll to flee: **a Death Knight breaks and runs.** Recall is **34 of 56 = 61 %** of the monsters the game's own naming calls Void or undead. **The risk was filed and never measured.** §3 option (b) of `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-play-01b-conquerors-hand.md` reads *"Zero data pass, but **fuzzy and can mis-tag**"* — chosen with the hazard written down, and nothing in the acceptance suite or any gate has looked at it since 2026-07-12. **Fix:** widen the name vocabulary to the standard undead set, exclude the `fish_*`/`night_*` pools from Void classification (a fish in a lake is a beast), and resolve the redundancy in §DX-02dj by either reading `voidTainted` as an override or retiring it. **Risk:** low mechanically, real on balance — tagging 22 more monsters Void removes 22 flee outs and adds 22 enrages, four at deadly magnitude, so pair it with a play pass. **Verify:** extend `src/tests/integration/enemy-ai.test.js` with a census assertion — the tagged count, plus a named allow/deny list pinning `fish_19` mundane and `death_knight` Void — so the vocabulary can never drift silently again.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bm — the failed-check effort grant is correct, consistent, and reaches 91 of 2,634 quests.** 🟡 **not a defect; a call the project has never made. Do not build before the call.** §XP-01 grants a failed skill-check `EFFORT_XP_PCT` of the reward the pass would have paid, read out of the quest's own `onPass` by `` `const rewardXp = ((sc.onPass@7007` `` → `` `const effXp = Math.round@7008` ``. **Census at HEAD** (`src/js/wbapi-core.js` `W.load`, 2,853 quests / 2,823 UQF): **2,634** carry a `skill_check` bit; **2,602 (98.8 %)** ship `onFail:[]` — §XP-01's premise, and stronger than the report's *"overwhelming majority"* hedge; but only **91 (3.5 %)** carry an `onPass` `reward` bit with `xp`, while **2,438 (92.6 %)** are `mission_bit`-only. Effort grants across the whole corpus run **6–125 XP**, median **44**, **4,067 XP** in total if every one of the 91 were failed once. **The design is internally consistent and that is the point:** those 2,543 checks pay no XP on *pass* either — they pay a token minted by `` `function _grantMissionBit@26110` ``, which awards none (`` `Token received: @26126` ``). §XP-01 did not miss them; the XP economy does not reach there. **Verified live** (throwaway probe, run and deleted): failing `quest_spark_01`, a mission-bit-only check, grants 0. **The open question:** should witnessing a moment be worth XP? **(a) Yes** — a flat grant inside the `mission_bit` opcode, the `` `const EXPLORE_XP@24436` `` precedent (bounded, one dial, no per-quest authoring), after which §XP-01's 25 % rule inherits 2,438 quests for free and the directive becomes true as written. **(b) No** — then *"all action earns XP"* is true of the combat and exploration loops only, and `mechanics.md` §XP plus `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md` should say so rather than stating the principle unqualified. **(a) is the one that matches the stated intent**, but it moves the whole level curve: 2,853 quests are calibrated against the present economy, so it needs the same balance pass §DX-02i refused to skip. **Risk:** none until built; high if built without the pass. **Verify:** whichever branch is chosen, pin it with a census assertion — the count of skill-check quests that can pay effort XP — so the number cannot drift silently again.

### §DX-02dc — `.info-chip.corpse-chip`: four CSS rules, zero applications, and a ship notice claiming it wired them (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cc, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02dc — a selector nothing has ever applied, announced as revived.** 🟢 **retirement sweep member; no design call.** Four rules carry the class — `` `.info-chip.corpse-chip { border-color: #a07840;@1317` `` and its `:hover` at 1318, plus `` `.info-chip.corpse-chip { border-left: 3px solid var(--border);@2906` `` and its `:hover` at 2907 in the light-theme block. **`git log -S "info-chip corpse-chip" --all` (no pathspec) returns 0 commits — the class has never been applied to an element anywhere in the repository's history**, on any branch, in any file. They were born dead in `213d14b` (§DESIGN-02, 2026-05-26). §DEATH-01 correctly identified them as *"a dead affordance"* and then built an **ID**-based chip beside them: `` `<div id="corpse-chip" style="display:none" onclick="storyMapToggle()">@4268` `` with its own rule at `` `#corpse-chip {@1759` ``, resolved by `` `function _renderCorpseChip()@36140` `` through `getElementById`. **That was the right build** — the location card is not an info-chip row — **but `a52f9cd`'s commit message states it *"Wires the long-dead `.corpse-chip` CSS"*, which is false**, and the tombstone was left standing next to its look-alike. This is §AUDIT-03aa's *"a comment asserting the removal of a symbol that still resolves"* inverted: **a commit asserting the revival of a selector nothing applies.** **Fix:** delete the four rules. **Why it belongs to the §DX-02bf/bi/bk/bl retirement sweep and not to housekeeping:** every dead-symbol census in the repo scans JS identifiers, and a CSS class selector is invisible to all of them — `check:deadconsts` cannot see this shape, and the two ID-vs-class near-twins ten lines apart in one stylesheet are exactly what a future reader will mis-read. Argues for extending the census to class selectors declared in the `<style>` block and never applied by `className` / `classList` / a literal `class="…"`. **Risk:** none (four unreferenced rules). **Verify:** `grep -c "info-chip corpse-chip"` stays 0 before and after; the chip still renders and still styles from `#corpse-chip`; `death-loot-grave` 4/4.

### §DX-02dd — the §DEATH-01 grave marker has two blind spots and the one assertion its own plan named was never written (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cc, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02dd — (a) the Local-grid grave marker does not paint on the player's own cell or on an unvisited cell, and has no test.** 🟢 **no design call.** §DEATH-01 Inc B marks the grave cell on the Local map: `` `const _graveHere = (S_story.corpsesQuests || []).filter@36706` `` → `` `cell.classList.add('mc-grave');@36709` `` inside `` `function _renderMapGrid()@36654` ``. **Proved in a real browser** (throwaway Playwright probe driving `_renderMapGrid()` against three planted corpse geometries, run and deleted): a visited in-window grave paints **1** `.mc-grave` cell with its 🦴 mark ✅; **the cell the player is standing on paints 0**; an in-window but **unvisited** node paints **0**. Both are structural — the marker sits inside the `else if (code)` → `if (isVis || isTrail)` branch, so the `isCurrent` arm (which paints `◉`) wins on the player's own cell and a third arm swallows unvisited ones. **Neither is a loss** — standing on the grave the node card's `` `_mkSection(null, '🦴', 'Remains')@35714` `` section carries both the signal and the retrieve action, and off-map the chip names the place — **but the map contradicts the chip beside it**, and the report's honesty ledger, which is otherwise exhaustive, declares neither case. **The reason both survived 37 days is (b):** §DEATH-01's verification plan promised *"Local-grid marker present on the grave cell when in view"*, and **`src/tests/integration/death-loot-grave.test.js` contains no `mc-grave` assertion; no spec in the repo references the class.** The one Inc-B surface touching the map renderer is the one surface with no coverage. **Fix:** apply the grave class in the `isCurrent` arm too (the ◉ cell can carry a 🦴 corner mark without losing the you-are-here glyph), decide explicitly whether an unvisited grave should mark — it is knowledge the player demonstrably has — and add the assertion the plan named. **Risk:** low, one render branch plus a test. **Verify:** the probe's three geometries return 1 / 1 / (declared value) and the new assertion pins them; `death-loot-grave` green.
- [ ] **§DX-02dd (b) — `S_story.corpsesQuests` is unbounded, and the deferral has been unfiled for 37 days.** 🟢 The only `splice` on the array is the *recovery* path at `` `function _storyRetrieveCorpse(questId)@26036` ``; there is no cap anywhere. Fifty deaths means fifty records persisted to `localStorage`, fifty journal rows under `` `hd.textContent = '☠ Fallen Hero — Corpse Retrieval';@30820` ``, fifty `nodeName`s concatenated into one `title` attribute at `` `el.title = 'Your remains lie out in the world@36149` ``, and a per-cell filter over the whole array on every Local-map render. Still minor and still not a loss — **the point is the provenance**: §DEATH-01's own honesty ledger wrote *"noted for a future soft-cap"* on 2026-07-12 and no row was ever filed, which is instrument 112's shape exactly (a correct, specific, scoped-out diagnosis is indistinguishable from an undiscovered defect until someone re-reads the report). **Fix:** a soft cap that merges or drops the oldest corpse past N, or an explicit decision that unbounded is correct — either is fine, but it should be written in a row rather than in a closed report's ledger.

### §DX-02de/df — §XP-01 ships the XP and withholds the telling: one notice is overwritten before it paints, one counted attack surface pays nothing (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cd, 🟡/🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02de — the failed-skill-check effort notice is written and has never been displayed.** 🟡 **born dead, not rot; one-line fix.** §XP-01's fail branch grants the XP correctly and then announces it: `` `The attempt was not wasted@7014` `` writes *"The attempt was not wasted. +N XP for the effort."* into `#story-move-msg`. Two statements later `_resolveQuestUQF` calls `storyRender(...)`, whose tail runs `` `storyMsg(parts.join@36025` `` **unconditionally** — and when the render yields no loot or quest message, `parts` is empty and the tail writes the empty string.
