The notice is destroyed in the same synchronous tick that created it, so it has never painted a frame. **Proved in a real browser** (throwaway Playwright probe driving the real `_resolveQuestUQF`, run and deleted): forcing `quest_muffat_01` to fail grants the correct **+38 XP** and leaves `#story-move-msg` reading empty. **`storyMsg(parts.join` is present at the parent build `8ae9e8c^`, so this never worked for one day of the 37** — a born-dead surface, not drift. **The sharpest part is that the same function was already repaired for exactly this hazard, and the repair stops nineteen lines short:** §BOARD-01-FU6 buffers the PASS branch through `` `let _passMsgs = null;@6988` `` and hands the text to `storyRender` as its `prefix` argument, under a comment naming the failure mode verbatim (*"storyRender's tail storyMsg overwrites #story-move-msg"*). The fail branch, added by §XP-01 nine days earlier, was left on the raw `storyMsg` idiom. **Player impact is pedagogical, not mechanical** — the XP arrives and `` `function _checkLevelUp@25672` `` still opens the modal — but §XP-01's whole job is to teach the player that failure is not a dead end, and the one sentence that teaches it is deleted before it can be read. **Fix:** buffer the string and pass it as `storyRender`'s prefix, exactly as the pass branch already does. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** a Playwright assertion on the *property* — force a reward-bearing check to fail, assert `#story-move-msg` carries the effort text after the render settles; `effort-xp` 3/3.

- [ ] **§DX-02df — the off-hand miss is counted and unrewarded, the one swing the engine tallies and refuses to pay for.** 🟢 **stated scope, unstated gap.** Story mode has exactly **two** `attacksAttempted` tally sites: the main attack inside `` `function _overlayPlayerAttack@25021` `` (granted, via `` `function _grantMissEffortXp@25010` `` in both miss branches) and the off-hand bonus-action swing inside `` `function _overlayOffhandAttack@25103` `` (**not** granted — its miss branch `` `Offhand NAT 1 — Miss@25120` `` sets a log line and returns). **Measured live in one encounter:** main-attack miss **+20 XP**, off-hand miss **+0 XP**, same enemy, same tick. §XP-01's §3 did say the helper touches *"only `_overlayPlayerAttack`'s two miss branches"*, so this is **stated scope rather than an oversight** — but its §1 opens by declaring that *missed attacks earn nothing*, and the user directive is *all action earns XP*. A dual-wielding player is left holding the exception, and the report never flags it. **A third surface has the same shape:** the free swing inside `` `function _storyFleeMutual@25700` `` (`` `const pHit  = pRoll@25705` ``) is neither tallied nor rewarded. **Blast radius is small by construction** — main-attack misses usually reach the per-encounter cap (`` `const cap  = Math.round@25012` ``) regardless, so the off-hand grant is frequently zero anyway; fix it for consistency, not for balance. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** extend `src/tests/integration/effort-xp.test.js` with an off-hand forced-miss case asserting a non-zero grant under the same cap, and re-assert that the cap still binds across both surfaces combined.

### §DX-02fu — two blocks the §VM-01-G-FU census counted and no slice claimed (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DOC-02db, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02fu — the §VM-01-G-FU-f ship record says "THE BLOCK INVENTORY IS COMPLETE"; measured at HEAD it is 44 of 47.** 🟢 Three `node.code ===` comparisons remain below the migration front in `storyRender`. One is the HW1 `whisperSaintSeen` latch, **deliberate** and documented inline (*"the migration moves surfaces; the latch has nothing to render"*). The other two were censused and then dropped by the slice plan's arc partition: **(1) `NWI` — the §SPARK-01 SEA Warmth Eel block**, a 3-state `.sweelinck-variant` panel (`story-ow-eel`, states: found / escorted / neither). It is named **first** in the §11 census table's multi-state row, and then G-FU-b took `KSU`/`ALF`, G-FU-c took `PDL`/`MLA`, G-FU-d took `SEN` and G-FU-e took `DA2`/`DA3` — nobody took `NWI`. **(2) `HKG` — the Layer 41 "Void Below" descend chip**, gated on `_npcFavor('crov') >= 1 && _npcFavor('auros') >= 1` + `quest_void_below` active + `!defeatedBattles['CY_VOID']`, an `info-chip battle-chip` inserted into the engine's own chip row. **Both are shapes the shipped vocabulary already expresses** — NWI is three `NODE_PANELS` entries in the DUS else-leg shape (sibling analysis required first, the G-FU-b WRO rule); the HKG chip is a chip-row surface and may be a `NODE_HOOKS` verbatim move. **No design call**; the value is closing the inventory claim honestly rather than leaving a "complete" that is 94%.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02db, findings D6 + F5.

### §DX-02fv — three surfaces the §VM-01-G4 design classified as choices and no slice ever took (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DOC-02dc, 🟡 ONE DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02fv — `lab-report-vm01g4-per-verb.md` §4 counts SEVEN D2 exclusive-choice surfaces; two are excluded by its own free-text rule, two shipped as the G4b pilot, and the remaining three were never given a slice — while the report's status block says "the slice plan is COMPLETE."** 🟡 Complete against §10 (which slices the pilot, D1 and D3); **not** complete against §4. The three, all still inline in `storyRender` at HEAD: the **Memory Gate** (`CO` catacombs approach, l. 34960 — two options: attempt the ceremonia via `_rollCeremonia('quest_d0204_a1')`, or force through for `S_story.hp = Math.max(1, (S_story.hp || 1) - 15)`), the **Prior Carrier** (`NUE`, l. 35009 — three options writing `priorCarrierSeen`/`priorCarrierSpoke`), and **TL Vonn** (l. 34840), which §9.3 named in as many words as *"the one true multi-step case — **the pilot for `choice`**"* and then routed to a later slice that never came. **The consequence is the measurement:** `kind:'choice'` occurs **exactly twice in the entire game**, both in `dus-kern-sable-first`/`-followup`@34298/34316 — so nineteen days and eleven further slices after the opcode got its host end, it still has **one consumer surface**. **Blocked-by, measured:** the Memory Gate needs §VM-01-G4c-FU **ask 3** (a bit that *starts* a ceremonia) and the Prior Carrier needs **ask 2** (panel chrome); **TL Vonn needs neither** — it is a plain nested two-step at a bare button and is buildable today. **ASK:** (a) slice TL Vonn now as `choice`'s second consumer and let the other two ride their asks, (b) hold all three until asks 2+3 are answered and ship them as one slice, or (c) record all three as deliberately-inline with a stated reason and correct the report's "COMPLETE" line. **Recommendation: (a)** — the opcode's whole value is that a branching beat becomes testable data, and one consumer is not evidence that it works; TL Vonn is the cheapest possible second, and the report already nominated it.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02dc, finding N3.

### §DX-02et — six world-progression notes are written into the movement breadcrumb array (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DOC-02cy, 🟡 one reader, ONE DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02et — `S_story.log` is the node-code trail, and Layer 44 posts journal entries into it.** 🟡 `S_story.log` has **eight** consumers. Six treat it as an array of node-code **strings**: `S_story.log.push(S_story.currentCode)@28360`, capped at twenty on the next line, read back as `new Set((S_story.log || []).slice(-20))` in three separate minimap renders plus `[...S_story.log, S_story.currentCode]@35913`. The other two `unshift` an **object** — `function _checkWorldProgressionEvents()@27585` writes `{ type:'world', text: ev.journalNote, day: … }`, and `@32249` does the same for one ambient note. **Nothing anywhere reads `.text` or `.type` off that array.** The `#journal-card` UI is Froberger's Journal, keyed on `entry.entryNum` — a different system. And the styling is stranded with it: `.journal-entry.world@2116` is a 5-line rule for a class the render path never applies; `document.querySelectorAll('.journal-entry')` returns **0** at every point in a run (measured in Chromium).
> **Six authored strings no player has ever seen:** *"Structural assessment submitted — Auros's name on the cover page."* · *"A courier delivered something to BA. Pachelbel signed for it."* · *"A letter arrived for Brynn at the inn. The seal is from the Heartwood district."* · *"City records show a new internal affairs submission. Source: anonymous. It wasn't anonymous."* · *"Weckmann added a second training session. Thursdays, younger fighters."* · *"The guard on the corner — Nivers. Eleven years."* The note also quietly occupies one of the twenty breadcrumb slots on its way to being ignored.
> **THE CALL — where should a world note go?** (a) **Route to the existing message stream** (`storyMsg`) with the `world` styling applied there — cheapest, visible immediately, but transient. (b) **Give the notes their own array** (`S_story.worldLog`) and a small panel or Journal tab that renders `.journal-entry.world` as designed — honors §III's *"the player may miss these entirely if they don't check the journal"*, which only works if there **is** a journal to check. **(b) recommended**; it is the design's stated intent and (a) contradicts §III's whole restraint premise. Either way the breadcrumb write must stop.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02cy, finding F2, `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-living-world.md` §III.

### §DX-02ew — the inn ledger prints +138 under a −8 balance, and reaches surplus a spec forbids (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DOC-02cy, 🟡 ONE DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02ew — Brynn's balance sheet does not close, and never did.** 🟡 The panel at `function _nodeHookBirkaBrynnMaintenance(node,@32136` prints `Room nights 235 · Meals 84 · Kitchen staff −45 · Supplies −112 · Repairs −24`, then a `BALANCE` line reading **−8 copper**. `235 + 84 − 45 − 112 − 24 = **+138**`; fixing the third step moves Repairs to −20 and the printed sum to **+142**. The `BALANCE` line is not computed from the lines above it — it renders `S_story.brynLedgerBalance`, a separate field initialised to `-8@23097`. **The lock had the same gap and the implementation reproduced it faithfully**, which is arguably the correct call for a design lock. Brynn's inn is, on its own printed evidence, comfortably profitable and being told otherwise.
> **The second miss is exact.** §VII promises *"The deficit can be brought to zero but not into surplus — Brynn keeps prices fair."* The three tasks add `+4`, `+2`, `+3` to a `−8` start. Measured through the tasks' own `action()` closures: `−4 → −2 → **+1**`. The engine even has a dedicated branch for the state the design forbids — `bal === 0 ? '0 copper (balanced)' : '+' + bal + ' copper'` — rendering that one copper in green. **A design lock in this same corpus (§DX-02en) missed its best ending by exactly one point; this one misses its promise by exactly one copper.**
> **THE CALL:** (a) **fix the promise, not the numbers** — change §VII's line to acknowledge a one-copper surplus (zero code); (b) **fix the surplus** — retune one task from +3 to +2 so the ceiling is exactly 0, honoring the lock; (c) **fix the ledger** — make the printed lines sum to the balance, which means re-authoring five figures and losing the joke. **(b) recommended** — one character, and Brynn keeps prices fair. Do **not** do (c) without a design call; the numbers are flavor, not simulation.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02cy, finding F6, `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-living-world.md` §VII.

### §DX-02ex — one boolean carrying two opposite meanings, and the reader that speaks absolution over neglect (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DOC-02cy, 🟡 one flag split)

- [ ] **§DX-02ex — `couperiDebtDegraded` means both "the debt got worse" and "the debt was released".** 🟡 Two writers disagree about what the field records. **Layer 44** (`const WORLD_PROGRESSION_EVENTS = [@27405`, id `quill_debt`) sets it when `actNumber >= 4 && !S_story.quillQuestComplete` — *the quest was never taken and the debt got worse* — correctly silent (`journalNote: null`, exactly as §III specifies). **Layer 69** (§XXXIV Beat 3, `@35292`) sets it at `MHQ` when `quillQuestComplete && !couperiDebtDegraded` — *the quest was finished and the debt was released* — announced with *"── The Couperin debt has done its work. ──"*.
> The two epilogue readers (`@28098`, `@28101`) are both gated on `quillQuestComplete`, so they only ever see Layer 69's meaning and stay coherent. **The dialogue reader does not.** `@23584` injects a release lesson into Quill's impartial pool on the bare flag, so a player who reaches Act IV having **never started** Quill's quest triggers Layer 44's writer and is then philosophised at about how *"a debt that has done its work becomes just a number. That's when you can release it"* — about a debt they left untouched. **The lock's own intended line for this state was quieter and correct:** *"The number is a number now. I don't look at it anymore."*
> **Fix:** split the flag (`couperiDebtDegraded` for neglect, `couperiDebtReleased` for Beat 3) and give `@23584` the lock's line for the neglect branch. **Save-compat:** the field is in `_S_DEFAULTS`; a migration that maps an existing `true` to the release meaning is correct for every run that completed the quest and wrong only for abandoned runs.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02cy, finding F8.

### §DX-02eo — a state field the spec named, created, wrote and never read; and a fourth epilogue key with no selector (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cx, 🟢 delete two lines + one branch, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02eo — `ebReturnsCompleted` is a write-only twin of `ebReturnDone`.** 🟢 Declared in `_S_DEFAULTS` (`journalEntriesRead: [], ebReturnsCompleted: {},@23087`), written on every Epic Battleground return (`S_story.ebReturnsCompleted[ebCode] = true;@30366`) — and read by **nothing**. Every consumer in the file (nine sites, incl. `function _buildEpilogueScroll()@28121`, `function _missionComplete()@23649`, the victory `vic-returns` stat) reads the sibling `ebReturnDone`. The Layer 43 spec gated its EB epilogue block on `ebReturnsCompleted >= 10`; the implementer built the field the spec named and then wired everything to the other one. **Fix:** delete the two lines that maintain it, or repoint one reader at it — not both. Grep first; it is in save files in the wild, so leave the `_S_DEFAULTS` slot if a save-shape test pins it.
- [ ] **§DX-02eo(b) — `FROBERGER_EPILOGUE.cursed` is unreachable.** 🟢 `const FROBERGER_EPILOGUE = {@27308` authors four states; `_buildEpilogueScroll` selects three (`covenant` / `imperfect` / `efficient`). `.cursed` was written for `!missionComplete && curse >= 15` — and that is exactly the state where the function **returns the Groundhog block early**, before the Froberger append is reached. The line (*"Froberger never finished his last entry… They did not stay long either."*) is the best in the set and has never rendered. Cheapest honest fix: append it to the Groundhog block's tail.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02cx, findings F4 + F5.

### §DX-02eq — a five-perk tree with an unlock, a message, a badge, and no mechanics (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cx, 🟡 implement-or-retire)

- [ ] **§DX-02eq — `_applyPitPerks` writes five flags and nothing reads them.** 🟡 `function _applyPitPerks(combatState)@28182` runs at `_showBattleOverlay` and sets `controlledAggression` · `readTheRoom` · `groundGame` · `cornerWork` · `crovsLesson` on the live combat state. **Each of those five names occurs exactly twice in the whole file** — once in `perkList` inside `function _checkPitPerkUnlock()@28170`, once in the assignment above. The duel engine never consults any of them, so every specified effect is inert: +1 to attack when flanking · pre-combat enemy HP tier · free shove on a crit · 1d4 HP between rounds at `HKG`/`LCY` · once-per-rest Recompose. The ceremony around them is complete and correct — sequential unlock at one win per perk, `🏆 PERK UNLOCKED` in Weckmann's voice, carry-over through NG+, and the `Weckmann's Student` badge on the character sheet at 5/5 — so the player earns a tree, is congratulated for it, sees it on their sheet, and it changes nothing.
> **`world.md` documented the no-op faithfully, which is why nobody caught it.** Its perk table's *"Combat effect"* column reads `combatState.groundGame = true` — the **write**, presented as the effect. Corrected in this increment with a measured note.
> **THE CALL — implement or retire?** (a) **Wire the two cheapest and retire the other three:** `readTheRoom` (show the threat tier the pre-battle overlay already computes) and `crovsLesson` (clear `battleDis` once per rest) are each a few lines against existing machinery; flanking, shove and between-rounds regen are genuinely new combat systems. (b) **Wire all five** — a real combat increment, needs its own row and its own tests. (c) **Retire the tree to flavour**: keep the unlocks and the badge, restate the table as Weckmann's teaching rather than mechanics, and delete `_applyPitPerks`. **(a) is the honest default** — it makes the last perk, *Weckmann's Lesson*, actually save your life, which is the only one the design document argues for by name.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02cx, finding F8, `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-endings-and-echoes.md` §IV/F8.

### §DX-02er — `Math.random()` decides where a key item lives, and one `→ doc:` pointer names a section that does not exist (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cx, 🟢 two lines + one comment, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02er(a) — the seeded-RNG fence has a hole in the ending arc.** 🟢 `S_story.frobergerNoteNode = _ebPool[Math.floor(Math.random() * _ebPool.length)];@23981` picks which of the 20 Epic Battlegrounds carries Froberger's Last Note, and the identical line runs again on the New Game+ path. Invariant #6 requires game-state randomness to draw the seeded stream (`_seededNext()`). Which node holds a key item is game state. The roll happens once at new-game time and is persisted, so a *save* still determines the future — the violation is narrow, but it is the same fence `check:rng` enforces elsewhere and it is two characters to close.
- [ ] **§DX-02er(b) — a doc pointer with no target.** 🟢 `const COVENANT_STANDING_LABELS = [@27357` carries `// → doc: docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md §Covenant Standing`. That file contains **zero** occurrences of the string; the live home is `story.md §Covenant Standing Tiers`, which `check:anchors` now resolves. Repoint the comment. *(Deferred out of §DOC-02cx because it edits `play.html`, whose working tree carries the user's uncommitted CSS recolor.)*
> **Detector wanted, and it is the cheap general one:** the file carries ~hundreds of `// → doc: <file> §<Section>` comments and **nothing verifies that the file exists or that the section heading is in it.** A gate that parses every `→ doc:` pointer and resolves `<file>` + `§<Section>` against the doc tree is a ~40-line script in the `check:anchors` house style, and it is the mirror of gate #15: anchors prove doc→code, this proves code→doc. Same class as §DX-02em's *"no gate proves every real node reaches a doc."*
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02cx, findings F9 + §VI.

### §DX-02ei — the Warrant's Board has never posted a job you could walk to (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cv, 🟡 the origin lock's predicate has no distance term, ONE DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02ei — over thirty consecutive game days at `TLL` the board posted 120 bounties; the nearest was 13 legs away, the median 23, the mean 30.2, and 115 of 120 (96%) were more than 20 legs out. Zero were within ten.** 🟡 **one design call, then a few lines in the selector.** `function _bountyPostable(q, node)@37131` gates on schema, type, a *distant* `activateNode`, not-already-started, destination-exists and both activation gates — **and nothing else**. `function _boardBounties(node, limit)@37165` then sorts the survivors on `(daySeed ^ _boardSeed(q.id + '|' + day))`, a hash that has never heard of geography. So the 1,081-candidate pool at a rest node is the *whole legal world*, and the four cards drawn from it are four uniform samples of it.
> **Measured, not argued** (Chromium at HEAD, node `TLL`, fresh game, days 0–29, reading FU3's own `~N legs` label off the rendered `.story-card-sub`): **n = 120 · min 13 · median 23 · mean 30.2 · max 79 · ≤5 legs: 0 · ≤10 legs: 0 · >20 legs: 115**. The identical slate renders at the ship build `8dcca67`, without the label — so this has been true since the feature shipped and no follow-up changed the selection.
> **Why it is not a coding bug, and why FU3 did not fix it.** Every guard in the predicate is correct. §BOARD-01-FU3 (`89133c4`) shipped the `~N legs` label, which tells the player the job is 22 legs away; **a label is not a filter**. The lock's own §8 walk-through imagines a local board — its worked example is `→ Visby Underground`, a neighbour — while §7 builds a global one. ***A board narrated as "the odd jobs at this inn" and selected as "every legal quest in the world" are two different features; the lock wrote the first and specified the second.***
> **DESIGN CALL — how local should the Warrant's work be?** (a) **Reserve local slots (recommended):** force the *k* nearest eligible candidates into the slate before the hash fills the rest — smallest change, keeps determinism, and makes §8's own example reachable. (b) **Bias the sort:** fold `_roadGridPath` length into `_k` as a tiebreak weight; costs a BFS per candidate, so it needs the §DX-02eh cost-bound comment fixed first. (c) **Cap by tier:** let Warrant standing widen the *radius* as well as the slate — a newcomer gets work within a day's walk, `Warrant's Own` gets the continent. Composes cleanly with §DX-02eg's reserve-a-slot fix. (d) **Accept it:** declare the board deliberately global (the Warrant is an international body) and change §8's prose instead. Cheapest, and defensible — but then the four cards a new player sees are all unreachable for hours.
> **Verify:** re-run the 30-day sweep and require a stated floor (e.g. ≥1 card ≤10 legs per day); `src/tests/integration/warrants-board.test.js` must keep its determinism and non-empty-slate assertions. Full measurement in `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-warrants-board.md` §8.

### §DX-02ej — the opt-in host flag has never been authored, so the node that motivated the board still has none (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cv, 🟢 one token of data and two allowlist entries, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02ej — `NODE_MAP[code].board` is concept C2 of the §BOARD-01 lock, half of its §5 data shapes, and it has ZERO authors at ship and ZERO at HEAD; the only occurrence of the string `board:` in `play.html` is the comment describing it.** 🟢 **one data token, plus two allowlist decisions.** Every one of the 38 hosts is a host because `sleep === true`; the opt-in and the suppression branches of `function _boardHost(node)@37020` have both been unreachable since the day they were written.
> **The consequence is at the report's own opening sentence.** §BOARD-01 §1 proof #1 is `NODE_MAP.LHR.text` — *"Notices flutter on the board by the fountain"* — the say/do gap the whole feature exists to close. **`LHR` carries `sleep:false`.** Measured in Chromium at the ship build and at HEAD: `_boardHost('LHR')` → **false**, and `storyRender(NODE_MAP.LHR)` renders no `story-board-section`. Thirty-two days and eight follow-ups later the start node still describes a notice board it does not have. ***The report retired one zero-author seam (`unlock`, 0 → 20 authors) by adding another, and the new one is still empty.***
> **Fix A (one token, no design call):** author `board:true` on `LHR`. It is exactly what C2 was built for, it closes the gap at the node used to prove the gap existed, and it is the feature's best possible first impression — a new player reads the fountain line and finds the board in the same panel. Guard the slate is non-empty at `LHR` in `src/tests/integration/warrants-board.test.js` (its existing DOM test asserts *"at a rest node"* and so cannot see this).
> **Fix B (two entries in `const BOUNTY_TYPES@37018`):** `craft` is in the allowlist with **0** top-level `QUEST_DB` entries at the pinned parent and at HEAD — the §4 census counted 12 `type:'craft'` strings that are inventory items nested in `reward` bits, not quests. Meanwhile **`hybrid` (13 quests at HEAD) is excluded and has never been named by §BOARD-01 or any of its eight follow-up reports.** Drop `craft`, and decide `hybrid` — a one-line question, not a design track. Board-eligible at HEAD is **2,763 of 2,853**; adding `hybrid` makes it 2,776.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02cv. Both halves are recorded with their measurements in `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-warrants-board.md` §3, §4 and §5.

### §DX-02eg — the reward-ceiling filters a pool of 1,076 and the player reads a slice of 7 (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cu, 🟡 half a shipped design call is invisible, ONE DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02eg — `if (_boardRewardXp(q) > tier.rewardCap) continue@37174` is correct code with no reach: in sixty consecutive game days at maximum Warrant standing, the board never once posts a bounty the tier-0 ceiling would have hidden.** 🟡 **design call 1 of §BOARD-01-FU7 shipped in two halves; the slate half works and the premium half cannot be felt.**
> **Measured, not argued** (Chromium, ship build `e0051a8` and HEAD, node `TLL`, fresh game — both builds agree on every figure). The candidate pool is **1,076**. **35 (3.3%)** carry any reward-xp; **5 (0.46%)** exceed the tier-0 cap of `rewardCap: 250@37057`. Those five sort to positions **265, 461, 828, 859, 887**. The player sees `const shown = out.slice(0, limit@37185` — at most **7**. Swept over 60 game days at `Warrant's Own`: **0** days show a premium bounty; **6** days show any reward-xp at all. Every card on the measured slate at every one of the five ranks scores `_boardRewardXp` = **0**, and its `rewardStr` is empty.
> **Why it is not a coding bug.** `function _boardRewardXp(q)@37071` is right, the `continue` is right, and the §9 test passes honestly. It is a **population** mismatch: the gate acts on the *eligible* set and the experience is the *shown* set, joined by `(daySeed ^ _boardSeed(q.id))`, a sort key that does not know about standing. Widening the slice never reorders it, so the four cards a newcomer sees are still the first four at every rank and new cards append at the bottom.
> **The tell was in the report all along.** The §9 ceiling test reads *"inspect the CANDIDATE POOL (huge limit so the slice cannot hide it)"* — the test author knew the slice hides it and tested around it. ***A filter and a slice are two different populations: measure a listing gate against what is SHOWN, never against what is eligible.***
> **DESIGN CALL — where to put the premium signal.** (a) **Reserve a slot:** at tiers with a lifted cap, force the highest-`_boardRewardXp` eligible candidate into the slate before the hash sort fills the rest. Smallest change that makes the promise true, and it keeps determinism. (b) **Bias the sort:** fold `_boardRewardXp` into the sort key weighted by tier, so premium work drifts up rather than appearing outright. Softer, harder to test. (c) **Author the signal:** ship the §10 `premium:true` opt-in so the pool has more than five premium entries — fixes the density, not the slice, and is the larger job. (d) **Retire the ceiling** and let standing gate slate size only, deleting `rewardCap` from `const WARRANT_TIERS = [@37056`. **Recommend (a)**, then (c) if the ladder is ever tuned again.
> **Verify:** re-run the 60-day sweep at `Warrant's Own` and require ≥1 premium-bearing day; `src/tests/integration/warrants-board.test.js:501` must keep its non-empty tier-0 guard. Full measurement in `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-warrant-standing.md` §8.

### §DX-02eh — a tier rung that changes nothing, and a cost-bound comment its own increment invalidated (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cu, 🟢 one number and one comment, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02eh — `Sworn` (min 12) carries the same `slate: 6` as `Trusted` (min 7), so five more completed bounties buy a rank name and nothing visible; and `run it ONLY over the ≤4 shown cards@37188` still asserts a bound §BOARD-01-FU7 raised to 7.** 🟢 **two independent one-line edits.**
> **Measured.** In Chromium at ship and at HEAD, the board at standing 7 and the board at standing 12 are **the same six cards in the same order**. `Sworn`'s only other effect is lifting the ceiling 500 → `Infinity`, which by §DX-02eg gates 5 quests out of 1,076. The lab report's own §8 walk-through names Marked, Trusted and Warrant's Own and **skips `Sworn`** — because there was nothing to narrate. *A ladder rung that produces no observable change is a rung the player climbs without being told they climbed it.*
> **Fix A (tunable numbers, the §5 table is explicitly declared tunable):** give `Sworn` `slate: 7` and move `Warrant's Own` to `slate: 8`, or raise `Sworn`'s `min` so the rung is not free. One line in `const WARRANT_TIERS = [@37056`. Re-run `src/tests/integration/warrants-board.test.js:501` — its assertions are monotone (`n7 <= n12 <= n20`) and hold under either edit.
> **Fix B (pure comment):** `run it ONLY over the ≤4 shown cards@37188` is a §BOARD-01-FU3 comment defending a `_roadGridPath` BFS cost bound. FU7 raised the bound to 7 and left the sentence; it still says `≤4` thirty-two days later. Rewrite to name the tier slate rather than a literal. **No gate can see this** — `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js:65` collects `.md` files only, the same blind spot §DX-02ef was filed for. **Verify:** `grep -c "≤4 shown cards" play.html` reaches 0.

### §DX-02ea — the index built to retire an O(n) scan kept an O(n) guard, and it is now 100% of the call (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cr, 🟢 hoist one call, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02ea — `function _questsByNode(nodeCode)@36997` opens with `Object.keys(QUEST_DB).length`, and that line costs more than everything else the function does put together.** 🟢 **hoist one revalidation call; no contract change.** §VM-01-F-FU replaced `storyCheckQuests`' full-corpus activation scan with an `activateNode → [quests]` `Map`, guarded against runtime injection by comparing `QUEST_DB`'s live entry count on every call — Option A, chosen precisely because it needs **zero injector cooperation**, and that reasoning is still correct. **Measured in Chromium this session on the rebuilt ship-day tree (`549d6b4`), median of 3 × 1,000 iterations, ms per activation pass at LHR:** old scan **0.398** · shipped path **0.110** · **the guard alone 0.111** · a guard-free `Map.get` + iterate **0.0003**. The bucket work is **three hundred times below the guard**. The shipped call is still Θ(2,853); the increment banked **3.6×** of an available **1,300×**, and its lab report's `O(2,850) → O(1)` framing hid the price.
> **And §VM-01-G3 doubled it.** The activation loop was extracted into `function _uqfActivateAtNode(node)@30138` so per-node UI could see same-arrival activations, and it is now called **twice per arrival** — from `function storyRender(node, prefix)@34549` before the render body, and again from `function storyCheckQuests(node)@30167`. Idempotent and correct; but that is ~0.22 ms of full-corpus work per arrival to look up a **median of 3** quests. **§DX-02cl would make it three calls**, since its fix is to copy this same guard into `function _questNodes()@36977`.
> **Not a player-visible red, and say so in the row.** 0.22 ms is well under a frame and no player will feel it. The row exists because the cost **scales with the quest corpus** — which is the exact tax the increment was written to retire — and because a cheap lookup is what makes the pass safe to call from more places, which authors have already started doing.
> **Fix (no design call, Option A's contract preserved):** hoist revalidation to once per render — a `_questsByNodeRevalidate()` called at the top of `storyRender`, with `_questsByNode` reduced to a pure `Map.get`. Same zero-cooperation guarantee, same staleness window, one guard per arrival instead of two or three. If §DX-02cl lands first, both indexes revalidate from that one call. **Verify:** `src/tests/integration/uqf-activatenode-index.test.js` 4/4 **unmodified** — the injection test is the whole point and must not be adapted · `quest-runtime-uqf` 303/303 · re-run the browser bench and assert the shipped path drops toward the 0.0003 ms floor.

### §DX-02dw — the one unguarded effect call in a kernel whose whole design rule is that guards live in the host (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cp, 🟢 one expression, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02dw — `QUEST:CORE` defends twelve of its thirteen injected effects and calls the thirteenth bare; a consumer that injects no `rng` gets a `TypeError` instead of the no-op the design promised.** 🟢 **one expression, no behaviour change on the live path.** §VM-01-D's §4 design call 2 is explicit — *"guards live in the host thunks, not the kernel"* — so that a server may inject real effects, no-op effects, or none at all. Twelve call sites honour it: `E.getQuest ? …`, `if (E.checkLevelUp)`, `if (E.mint)`, `if (E.preBattle)`, `if (E.setFavor) return`, and `mission_bit(bit, ctx)@22304` even carries an explicit `ctx.state` fallback. **The thirteenth is the die roll:** `` `const d20  = Math.ceil(E.rng() * 20)@22249` `` inside `function createQuestRuntime(host) {@22181`. **The report also claims a fallback that was never written** — §4 states *"a `Math.random()` fallback exists only for a server that injects no rng"*; the kernel contains **zero** `Math.random()` occurrences at the ship commit `9f10bfe` and at HEAD (measured this session). The zero is the better outcome for reproducibility; the missing guard is the defect.
> **Not hypothetical — a shipped, green, in-`check:walk` consumer already does this.** `src/scripts/check-gate-parity.js:const rt = Q.createQuestRuntime@32` builds a runtime with `effects: { getQuest: () => q }` and no `rng`, and survives only because gate evaluation never reaches `_rollSkill`. It is one `skill_check` walk away from a crash, and §VM-01-E's soft-lock work is precisely the direction that would take that walk.
> **Fix — pick one and say it out loud:** (a) guard it the way its siblings are guarded (`E.rng ? Math.ceil(E.rng() * 20) : …`) — but a silent default RNG in a kernel built for reproducibility is arguably worse than a crash, so (b) **preferred:** keep it required and make the contract loud — throw a named error from `createQuestRuntime` when `effects.rng` is absent, and correct §4 of `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-vm01d-quest-core-parity.md`, whose promised fallback does not exist. **Verify:** `npm run check:questparity` (the block is mirrored — edit `src/js/quest.js` and re-inline, §6.5) · `npm run check:gateast` · `src/tests/integration/uqf-quest-core.test.js` 7/7 · `quest-runtime-uqf` 303/303.

### §DX-02dx — the increment credited as the kernel's first consumer imports it and never calls it (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cp, 🟢 one line, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02dx — `src/scripts/check-questgraph.js:const Q = require@63` pulls in `src/js/quest.js`, and `Q` occurs exactly once in the file: on that line.** 🟢 **delete the import or use it; either way one line.** The file's own header comment advertises the dependency — *"the kernel via `require('../js/quest.js')`"* — and the §VM-01-C ship record credits §VM-01-E's static walker as a consumer of the §VM-01-D substrate. Measured this session: the walker re-implements what it needs against its own string/comment-aware brace matcher (`function matchBrace(src, openIdx)`) and never touches the kernel. **The genuine first consumer is §VM-01-F's** `src/scripts/check-gate-parity.js:const rt = Q.createQuestRuntime@32`, which runs the real `canActivate`/`canComplete` against an independently re-implemented reference interpreter — 76 assertions, green. §VM-01-D's §6 named two candidate first consumers (a future §MESH increment, or E's walker) and **neither is the one that arrived**; the substrate paid off through a door that was not on the list.
> **Why it is worth a line rather than a shrug.** A dead `require` of a parity-fenced kernel is a false positive for exactly the question this track keeps asking — *does the requireable kernel have consumers yet?* — and it is the reason the §VM-01-C record credits the wrong increment. `check:questgraph` is a **real** payoff of D (2,853 quests analysed, 2,804 reachable, 0 residual nondeterminism) and does not need a fictitious import to earn it.
> **Fix:** drop line 62 and the header clause, **or** route the walker's gate evaluation through `Q.createQuestRuntime` — the second is the better end state and is not free, so it is a follow-up, not this row. **Verify:** `npm run check:questgraph --selftest` then the full run; unchanged output either way.

### §DX-02dy — fourteen gates are still closures, in the increment whose whole premise is that gates are data (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cq, 🟡 one gate leaf, ONE DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02dy — `gate:{_legacyFn:true}` appears on 14 quests, and `src/scripts/check-questgraph.js:function gateSat@273` judges every one of them reachable by fallthrough.** 🟡 **one new gate leaf plus 14 one-token gate edits.** §VM-01-E opens by resting its whole method on §VM-01-F having made gates **data** — *"a normal model-checking problem now that gates are data"*. Measured this session at the ship-day build `354b20a^` **and** at HEAD, both **14** (a bare `grep -o` says 15; the fifteenth hit is inside the comment that explains the escape, so use the parser — instrument 51): `quest_wm_05` · `quest_muffat_01` · `quest_road_damascus` · `quest_lame_lystra` · `quest_areopagus` · `quest_ephesus_riot` · `quest_shipwreck_melta` · `quest_inn_06` · `quest_fish_01` · `quest_tour_01` · `quest_lxvii67` · `quest_guide_01` · `quest_courier_release` · `quest_crypt_survey`. `gateSat` recognises `all`/`any`/`not`/`flags`/`flagsAny`/`flagEquals`/`questsDone`/`questsComplete`/`questsAttempted`/`flagsPath` and then `return true`, so an unrecognised `_legacyFn` key is **unconditionally satisfiable**.
> **This is sound, and that is exactly why it needs a row.** The fallthrough over-approximates in the same direction as the monotone bound, so the *unreachable* verdict stays valid — the prover never invents a soft-lock. But it means the prover **cannot see 14 activation conditions at all**, and it reports nothing about them, so the blind spot is invisible in the output. A soft-lock hiding behind a `_legacyFn` gate would be reported as reachable, forever.
> **The cause is named in the file, and it is §VM-01-E Finding 1's real justification.** `is not expressible in canActivate@13974` reads: *"lxvii67's faith_folk>=1 gate is not expressible in canActivate's term set ⇒ gate:{_legacyFn:true}"*. The gate grammar has no **counter comparison**. Finding 1 argued for a `counter` opcode on the grounds that the tracks it writes *"are read by real gates (11–26 refs each)"* — **measured false this session: zero quest gates read `faith_folk`, `faction_hansa`, `voidPressure`, `tribbleCount` or `mazeSolvedChecks`, at ship-day or at HEAD.** They are not read by gates *because they cannot be*. The tracks are live all the same — `voidPressure` has real host readers — so this is a missing gate leaf, not dead data.
> **CORROBORATION (added 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cs) — the grammar has already grown a comparison term once, as a hard-coded special case.** §VM-01-F shipped the `{all}`/`{any}`/`{not}` gate AST on 2026-07-22 promising *"a new OR shape now goes in the grammar, never a new single-use term — the increment DELETES vocabulary."* Measured at its ship build and at HEAD: activation leaf terms **15 → 17**, `check:gateast` **72 assertions / 25 terms → 76 / 27**, and the two new terms are **`dayMin` / `dayMax`**, added by `37f8ccb` (§BOARD-01-VOID-GATE) **the next day**, 23 h 54 min later. The kernel comment says why: *"a clock gate the grammar can now express, so no per-quest `_legacy_fn`."* **They are not an OR shape — they are a comparison shape, and the combinators cannot make one.** `{all}`/`{any}`/`{not}` *compose* predicates; they cannot *create* one. `dayMin`/`dayMax` is a `counterMin`/`counterMax` for exactly one hard-coded counter (`st.day || 1`), which is this row's fix implemented for a single track. Two special-cased comparison terms shipping within a day of the grammar landing, plus 14 quests still on `_legacyFn` a month later, is the strongest available evidence that the general leaf is the right shape. **Add it to the verify list:** after `counterMin`/`counterMax` land, `dayMin`/`dayMax` should be expressible as `{countMin:[{path:'day',min:21}]}` + a max, and whether to retire them or keep them as sugar is a second, smaller call.

> **Fix / DESIGN CALL:** add a `counterMin` (and probably `counterMax`) leaf to the gate term set beside the existing `favorMin`/`shardsMin`/`restedAtMin` family, teach `gateSat` to treat it as monotone-satisfiable, then convert the `_legacyFn` gates that are pure counter comparisons. **The call is how many of the 14 are counter comparisons versus genuinely arbitrary** — that needs reading all 14 closures, and the answer decides whether this retires the escape hatch or merely shrinks it. **Verify:** `check:gateast` gains leaf assertions; `check:questgraph` reports the converted quests through real gate terms; the `_legacyFn` gate count drops.

### §DX-02do — the report proved there is no free-text input, then two of its own named features took free text and rendered it as markup (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02cj, 🟡 escape one sink, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02do — a player who writes `<` or `&` into Entry 42 silently corrupts their own journal entry, permanently.** 🟡 **`§DX-02bt` sibling, one class sharper: the string is player-TYPED, not author-authored.** `lab-report-javascript-mud.md` closed the injection question with an absolute — *"There is no free-text input anywhere in the story UI — nothing player-typed reaches the DOM at all"* — after checking exactly **one** function (`function storyCreateCustomQuest@38112`, which is genuinely safe: it constrains a `<select>` to a `WORLD_DB` key). **Instrument 131 — a negative about a resource is a census of its WRITERS, and the census finds two**, both inside `function storyRender(node, prefix)@34549`, both in the special-case region that same report devotes its §VII to, and **both named by the report in its own §VII diagram**: `` `entry42-textarea@34636` `` (*"Write Entry 42, or leave this blank."*) and `` `sg-secret-input@34767` `` (*"Type your secret here..."*).
> **The chain, measured end to end.** `` `entry42-write-btn@34635` `` reads `.value` → `S_story.entry42Text` → `storyAutoSave()` → **`localStorage`** → `function storyJournalToggle@30655` → `` `entry42Text.replace@30681` `` converts `\n` to `<br>` (the tell that the sink is markup) → `` `e42JovDiv.innerHTML@30683` ``. **Executed in a browser at BOTH the parent build `43bd09c` and HEAD, identically:** with `entry42Text` set to an `<img src=x onerror=...>` payload the tag **materialised as a real DOM element and the handler RAN**. Not a painted branch — proven live in both builds, so this was true the day the report was written.
> **Scope it honestly — this is NOT a security row.** Single-player local file: the only person who can type into that box is the player, and the only DOM they reach is their own, so the report's *conclusion* about the `_mkCard` `main` path stands. **The defect is content fidelity, in the one feature whose entire point is the byline *"by you"*.** Entry 42 is the game's single shipped inner-arc mechanic (§INNER-01 names it *"the proof the shape works and the template for more"*), it is carried across NG+, and it gates the fifth ending — so the one page the player is invited to author is the one page that eats their angle brackets and keeps the damage in the save file forever.
> **Fix (one sink, no design call):** build the entry with `textContent` per line instead of `innerHTML`, or escape before interpolating — the `\n` → `<br>` intent is preserved either way. Then re-check `` `sg-secret-input@34767` ``, whose text is trimmed into `S_story._voidTollSecret` and should be given the same treatment on the same pass. **Verify:** type `<b>x</b> & y` into Entry 42, reload, open the journal — the entry must read back exactly what was typed.

### §DX-02dp — the transient-DOM contract is written in sibling order, two hand-copied sweeps enforce it, and live features deliberately write into the gap (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02cj, 🟢 one wrapper div deletes two loops, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02dp — *"everything physically between `#story-text-box` and `#story-info-row` is transient"* is expressed in neither a class, an attribute, nor a container — only in sibling position.** 🟢 **filed 36 days late: `lab-report-javascript-mud.md` finding 11 proposed the fix on 2026-07-16 and it was never entered here** — `story-dynamic` appears nowhere in the repository except that one sentence. Two hand-written sweeps walk `nextElementSibling` and `.remove()` until they hit the info row, and **both are still live at HEAD**: one in `function storyRender(node, prefix)@34549` and its twin in `function _renderNodeShell@28409`. Two copies of one rule that can silently disagree — the shape §VM-01-G-FU-f2 retired for the final-battle predicate.
> **It has already cost one crash, and it is load-bearing today.** The `_renderNodeShell` copy carries a comment recording that a §MATH-01 change once wrote to a `#story-content` that did not exist and *"threw 'Cannot set properties of null' on every empty-cell step."* And the contract is not merely tolerated but **relied upon**: the Entry 42 prompt (§DX-02do) is inserted with `insertAdjacentElement('afterend')` on `#story-text-box` precisely so the next render sweeps it away. Any element any future author puts in that gap is deleted on the next step, by anyone, forever — and nothing in the markup says so.
> **Fix:** wrap the gap in `<div id="story-dynamic">`, render into it, and replace both loops with one `innerHTML = ''`. The contract becomes a container instead of a convention, and the second copy stops being able to drift from the first. **Verify:** step onto an empty cell and back to a named node — the Entry 42 prompt and the travel strip must still clear exactly as they do now.

### §DX-02dk/dl — the Void pressure meter, not the day counter, is the loss condition — and the opening screen describes the wrong one (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cg, 🔴/🟡 one design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02dk — a level-1 character can lose the game on Day 1 by walking, and the courier's frame promises they cannot.** 🔴 **the only §PLAY-01 finding that can end a run; copy fix or cap, one design call.** §PLAY-01-C reframed the deadline as generous on the strength of one measurement in `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-play-01c-no-postponements.md` §1: *"`voidPressure` rises **only** when you sleep into a scheduled Void-Tide day."* **There are five writers, and the one it names is the one that cannot kill you.** The tide schedule (`` `const VOID_TIDE_EVENTS@22369` ``) holds **7 days** — 3 · 7 · 14 · 21 · 28 · 35 · 42 — each worth `+1` through `` `_addVoidPressure(1);@36368` ``, so **sleeping through all forty-nine days tops out at 7 against a defeat threshold of 10**, before `` `S_story.void_mercy_count--@36348` `` subtracts from it. The path that *does* reach 10 is `` `function storyCheckMissedSleep@36357` ``, which fires `` `_addVoidPressure(1);@36368` `` every **second** sleep-capable node the player stands on **without resting** (`` `S_story.missedSleeps >= 2@36364` ``). A real-parser census (`src/js/wbapi-core.js` `W.load(GAME)`) counts **38 nodes with `sleep:true` out of 416**, so the exhaustion path alone offers **19 points — nearly double what it takes to lose — and costs zero days.** **Proved in a real browser** (throwaway Playwright probe, run and deleted): a fresh character who never sleeps reaches `voidPressure` **10/10 after 20 nodes**, `story-defeat-modal` opens with *"☠ The Void Has Breached"*, and its own scoreboard reads **"Day reached — Day 1."** The day counter never moved. **What shipped onto the opening screen says the opposite:** `` `Take the <b>time you need</b> — the seals will hold a long while yet@4953` `` and the chip tooltip at `` `Return all 7 Codex Shards, reach Level 20, and defeat Commander Auros@4260` `` both promise *"the Void gains only slowly, and only while you sleep"* — while the engine's own exhaustion line at `` `storyMsg('😴 Exhausted — DIS on next 2 battles. Void pressure rising. Sleep soon.@36370` `` tells the player to **sleep soon**. ***Two player-facing strings, one game, opposite instructions about the same mechanic*** — the say/do gap §PLAY-01-C was convened to close, re-created one screen over. **The game already owns an honest doom gauge and nobody framed it:** `` `voidEl.className = 'inv-stat-val' + (S_story.voidPressure >= 7 ? ' danger'@36091` `` paints the Void meter amber at 4 and red at 7 against the real threshold of 10 — the increment spent itself calming the counter that was never dangerous. **Fix (choose one):** (a) correct the two copy surfaces to name pressure rather than sleep — cheapest, and finishes the honesty job §PLAY-01-C started; (b) cap the exhaustion contribution below 10 so the framing becomes true; (c) give the Void meter the generous-horizon treatment the day chip got. **Recommend (a) plus a one-line cap review.** **Risk:** low for (a), balance-touching for (b). **Verify:** extend `src/tests/integration/courier-map.smoke.test.js` with an assertion that the frame text does not claim sleep is the sole pressure source, plus a probe asserting the exhaustion path is survivable from a fresh game.
- [ ] **§DX-02dl — three writers bypass the pressure helper, and the engine's last-chance warning is an exact-equality test in a system with a `+3` writer.** 🟡 **two independent one-line classes; no design call.** `` `function _addVoidPressure(n) {@26976` `` is the intended write path: it clamps at `` `S_story.voidPressure = Math.min(10, prev + n);@26979` ``, latches three narrative milestones, and refreshes the HUD. **Three sites write the field directly and skip all of it** — two quest `onFail` handlers at `` `onFail:[{ kind:'_legacy_fn', fn:() => { S_story.voidPressure@21514` `` and `` `onFail:[{ kind:'_legacy_fn', fn:() => { S_story.voidPressure@21536` ``, and the Ceremonia column claim at `` `S_story.voidPressure  = (S_story.voidPressure@31867` `` (`+3`). **Proved live:** set to 9 and apply the direct `+3` shape → **12**, where the helper clamps to 10; neither the milestones nor `storyUpdateStatus()` fire, and the defeat check is caller-side (`` `if ((S_story.voidPressure || 0) >= 10) { storyVoidDefeat@36353` `` and `` `if (S_story.voidPressure >= 10) { storyVoidDefeat@36369` ``), so an over-threshold player keeps playing until the next tide or exhaustion tick. **Second, independent defect:** `` `if (cur === 9 && !S_story.voidImminentWarned) {@26983` `` is an **exact-equality** milestone. Any step of 2 or more walks over it — **proved live**, `_addVoidPressure(8)` then `_addVoidPressure(3)` lands on **10 with `voidImminentWarned` still false**, so the *"⚠️ THE VOID IS IMMINENT — Pressure: 9/10"* warning and its shard-count mercy note never fire on the path most likely to need them. ***A milestone written as `===` is a milestone that assumes every writer steps by one; this system has a writer that steps by three.*** **Fix:** route the three direct writes through `_addVoidPressure`, and change `cur === 9` to `cur >= 9` (the `voidImminentWarned` latch already prevents repeats). **Risk:** low — the latch makes `>=` idempotent, and routing through the helper is strictly more correct at every call site. **Verify:** `grep -c 'S_story.voidPressure *=' play.html` drops to 1 (the clamp itself); a probe asserting an 8→+3 step fires the imminent warning; `courier-map` green.

### §DX-02dg/dh — §PLAY-01-A shipped the goal whole; a design reversal landed on one of its two surfaces and its one state field went into the wrong object (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02ce, 🟡/🟡 one design call)

@36175` ``); the sidebar still carries the original doom ladder (`` `(S_story.day >= 42 ? @36087` ``). **Proved in a real browser** (throwaway Playwright probe sweeping nine days across both surfaces, run and deleted): Day 34 both plain · **Day 35–41 sidebar amber, chip plain** · **Day 42–45 sidebar RED, chip plain** · Day 46–49 sidebar red, chip amber. **The two surfaces agree on no day above 34** — at Day 42 the sidebar screams and the chip introduced to explain the sidebar says nothing. §PLAY-01-C's commit carries an explicit *"Not touched (deliberately)"* list (the Day-49 defeat flavour, the Void-Tide events, `_addVoidPressure`, sleep, the day counter) and **the sidebar's colour is on none of it** — not a deferral that rotted, a surface that was never in view. ***A reframe applied to one surface is not applied to the feature*** — the sibling of §DX-02de's *a hazard fixed in one branch of a function is not fixed in the function*. **Fix:** align `#s-day` to the chip's framing (the reframe was a user decision, so the sidebar is the half that should move), or consciously revert the chip. **(b) The level pill warns you for being level 8 of 20.** `` `(lv >= 8 ? @36123` `` turns `#s-level` amber at Lv 8 — a threshold inherited from the initial commit `b7280b3`, when 8 was near the top of the curve. `XP_LEVELS` now runs to 20 and the chip directly beside it renders `⭐ Lv N/20`, so the sidebar flags **60 % of the levelling curve** as a warning state immediately below a chip stating that 20 is the target. **(c) The uncollected shard symbols measure 1.40:1.** The chip paints its own `rgba(20,14,4,0.55)` panel over the `#F0E6C8` parchment card; measured contrast against that blend is **3.12:1** for a collected 🔮, **2.54:1** for the leg text and **1.40:1** for an uncollected ◇ (`` `#objective-chip .obj-shard.empty@1750` ``). **This is as-designed and nothing drifted** — *"faded"* and *"dim"* are §PLAY-01-A's own words and `--bg` was parchment at the ship commit — but a feature whose entire thesis is *count what is left at a glance* should be legible at a glance. A designer's call on one alpha value. **Risk:** low, all three are one-liners. **Verify:** extend `src/tests/integration/courier-map.smoke.test.js` with a two-surface assertion — at any given day, `#s-day` and `#obj-day` carry the same severity class; `courier-map` + `navigation` green.
