' warn' : '');@36175` `` reads `` `const SHARD_GOAL = 7, LEVEL_GOAL = 20, DAY_DEADLINE = 49;@36157` `` — while the sidebar's is *hardcoded* at `` `dayEl.className = 'stat-val' + (S_story.day >= 42 ? ' danger' : S_story.day@36087` ``. They did not merely drift apart once; the next time the cap moves they will drift again, and only one of them will follow. **(2) The commit deleted the only assertion that guarded the invariant.** At the parent build `ac651a3`, `src/tests/integration/courier-map.smoke.test.js` carried the comment *"day threshold colouring agrees with the sidebar (danger at >= 42)"* and an `out.dayDanger` check enforcing exactly the agreement §PLAY-01-A had locked. `caa489e` removed the comment and the assertion **in the same hunk that broke the agreement**, and the test file has not been edited since (`git diff caa489e HEAD` over it is empty). ***The regression that guarded the invariant was retired by the change that violated it*** — which is the complete answer to the 37 days. **(3) Interval corrected:** the gap from `b46d3f0` to `caa489e` is **+19 min 42 s**, not the +2 h 40 m this row and `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-play-01a-courier-map.md` previously carried.

- [ ] **§DX-02di — half of the enemy-AI classifier is a constant-false predicate, and has been since the day it shipped.** 🟡 **live const, live reader, unreachable outcome — a gate class no existing check can see.** `` `function _isVoidEnemy@25146` `` decides press-vs-flee from two independent tests: Void/undead vocabulary in the monster name or key, **or** a Void-ish current terrain. The terrain half has two call sites, both matching `` `const _VOID_TERRAIN_RE@25145` `` against the node terrain (`NODE_MAP[*].name` — the terrain **key**, per the repo naming rule) and against `` `function _inferTerrain@28384` ``, which returns from that same vocabulary plus the literals `ocean`, `road` and `midlands`. **Measured:** the regex matches **0 of 111 `WORLD_DB` terrains** and **0 of the 109 terrain keys in use on nodes**. **Proved in a real browser** (throwaway Playwright probe, run and deleted): a mundane monster stood at **all 416 nodes** classifies Void at **none** of them, and `_inferTerrain` swept across the 90×360 grid returns **47 distinct terrains, 0 void-ish**. The two vocabularies were minted independently and **share not one word** — the regex asks for `graveyard`, `tomb`, `blight`, `wither`, `abyss`, `necro`, `dread`, `ash`, `void`, `corrupt`; the world spells its decay `catacombs`, `crypt`, `sewers`, `ruins`, `monster_cave`, `vampire_castle`, `drowned_shore`, `sunken_hall`, `fog_bank`, `cosmic_realm`. **Why nothing caught it:** the acceptance test's own comment claims to cover *"classification heuristic (name/terrain)"*, but all three of its assertions exercise the name half, and its single terrain touch is the **negative** case (*"rat at Birka city — not void"*) — which passes **because** the branch is dead. No test in the file can distinguish a working terrain branch from an unreachable one. ***This is a new member of the §DX-02 through-line and it is not §DX-02n or §DX-02y: the const is read, the reader is called on every enemy turn, and what is dead is the OUTCOME.*** A `check:deadconsts` scoped to unread symbols cannot see it; catching it needs a regex's alternatives intersected with the roster it is tested against. **Fix (choose one):** delete the terrain half as never-functional, or give it a terrain list drawn from the live roster — note that either choice changes behaviour, since today the terrain half contributes exactly nothing. **Risk:** low to delete, real to enable (see §AUDIT-03bn's balance note). **Verify:** an assertion that `_VOID_TERRAIN_RE` matches at least one live `WORLD_DB` key, or that it is gone; `enemy-ai` 4/4 green.
- [ ] **§DX-02dj — the alignment field the design ask proposed inventing already existed, on two monsters, and nothing reads it.** 🟢 **Hazard #2; two lines of data with zero consumers.** §1 of `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-play-01b-conquerors-hand.md` states flatly *"No alignment/kind field exists"* — **false the day it was written.** `voidTainted:true` was already on `` `ac:13, hp:28,  atk:5,  dmgDie:8,  dmgCount:1, dmgFlat:3@5652` `` (`void_wolf`) and `` `ac:12, hp:18,  atk:4,  dmgDie:4,  dmgCount:2, dmgFlat:0@5653` `` (`void_rat_swarm`) at the report's parent build, and `git log -S "voidTainted" --all` (no pathspec) traces it back through fifteen commits to `1b84f1f`, long predating §PLAY-01-B. So §3 option (a) — *"a **new** per-monster `void:true` field on the ~30–40 Void-aligned entries … but a data pass"* — proposed building something that existed and was **2/398 populated**; the user declined the data pass and chose the heuristic. **`_isVoidEnemy` does not read the field** (proved live: the token does not occur in the function source). Both tagged monsters classify Void anyway, **by name**, so there is no gameplay defect today — but the repo carries two competing answers to one question and consults only one of them, and the next author to add a Void monster has no way to know which surface is authoritative. `void_rat_swarm` is already the subject of §DX-02h. **Fix:** either read `voidTainted` first in `_isVoidEnemy` (an explicit override beating the heuristic, which also gives §AUDIT-03bn a clean escape hatch for the mis-tagged fish), or delete the field from both entries. **Risk:** none either way at 2 entries. **Verify:** `npm run check:dupkeys` + `enemy-ai` 4/4; if the override lands, pin `void_wolf` Void-by-field with the name regex stubbed out.
- [ ] **§DX-02dh — `courierMapSeen` was added to the seed literal instead of `_S_DEFAULTS()`, so the opening frame is a once-per-page-load feature and a defeated player never sees it again.** 🟡 **false at its own ship commit; one-line fix.** §PLAY-01-A's lab report §3 states the field is *"defaulted in `_S_DEFAULTS()` (§STATE-INIT single-source rule) so a fresh load and a reset agree."* **It is not.** It landed at `` `courierMapSeen: false,@23045` ``, inside the block whose own comment two lines above reads `` `seed only — replaced with the canonical@23000` `` … *"Do not rely on these values; keep `_S_DEFAULTS()` authoritative"* — and that seed object is discarded wholesale at `` `S_story = _S_DEFAULTS();@23404` ``. `` `const _S_DEFAULTS = () => ({@23063` `` has never carried the key, in any build, under any name. **The consequence is the guard running backwards.** `storyNewGame` resets through `` `Object.assign(S_story, _S_DEFAULTS());@23955` ``, which cannot clear a key its source object does not declare, so `courierMapSeen` is set `true` on the first fresh game and **never returns to `false` without a page reload**. **Proved in a real browser** (throwaway Playwright probe, run and deleted): `_S_DEFAULTS()` `hasOwnProperty('courierMapSeen')` = **false** · post-boot `S_story` = **false** · `storyNewGame()` #1 frame visible = **true** · `storyNewGame()` #2 in the same page session = **false**. **The reachable path is the defeat screen:** `` `, storyNewGame);@38311` `` wires the *"↩ New Game"* button straight to `storyNewGame` with no reload in between, so a player who loses and restarts in place is handed the world **without the map** — the only surface that states the win condition in words is gone, and four glyphs are left to carry it. The player most in need of the goal restated is the one guaranteed not to get it. The report called the guard *"belt-and-suspenders… so a future caller can't double-fire it"*; the belt held perfectly and the suspenders were nailed to the floor. **Why 37 days:** `` `src/tests/integration/courier-map.smoke.test.js:storyNewGame(scores);@18` `` calls it **once per page load**, so the second-game case is outside the test's universe. **Fix:** move the field into `_S_DEFAULTS()` (one line; the seed-literal copy may stay or go, the seed is discarded either way). **Risk:** low — a save still wins, because loads run `Object.assign(S_story, _S_DEFAULTS(), save)`. **Verify:** add an assertion driving two consecutive `storyNewGame()` calls in one page and asserting the frame opens both times; `courier-map` green.

### §DX-02cl — `_questNodes()` has no cache invalidation while the sibling index one function below has one, and explains why (NEW 2026-08-17 during §DOC-02bt, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02cl — §NAV-01's own follow-up #2 was answered by §VM-01-F-FU and never applied upward.** 🟢 **one guard line, ~10 minutes.** `function _questNodes@36977` builds the set of quest-offering node codes once per session (`if (!_questNodeSet)`) and never rebuilds it; its only consumer is the ❗ marker on the map (`if (_questNodes().has(code)) return '❗';`), so a quest injected at runtime shows no marker until reload. §NAV-01's lab report filed exactly this as open follow-up #2 on 2026-07-03 (*"invalidate if QUEST_DB ever mutates live"*) and it has been open 46 days. **The answer is already in the file, thirteen lines below:** `_questsByNode` (§VM-01-F-FU) rebuilds whenever `Object.keys(QUEST_DB).length` changes, with a comment naming the three live injectors — the test harness, the worldbuilder editor, and a future §MESH-delivered quest — and stating that the size guard *"catches every such add/remove with NO injector cooperation."* **The shipped game is unaffected** (`QUEST_DB` is a frozen const), so this is authoring-surface correctness, not a player-visible red — the same reason it survived a year of nobody noticing. **Fix:** give `_questNodes()` the identical entry-count guard; the two functions then share one invalidation story instead of two. **Risk:** none (the rebuild is the existing body). **Verify:** a scratch spec that injects a quest into `QUEST_DB` at runtime and asserts the ❗ marker appears at its `activateNode` without a reload.

### §DX-02bz — the fishing card advertises an hour it has never charged, and the game's two clocks disagree (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bl, 🟡 small design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02bz — `⏱ 1 hour · fishing session` is a label with no writer behind it, in a game whose central pressure is a 49-day clock.** 🟡 The fishing card's hint on line 35371 promises an hour. **`S_story.hoursElapsed` has exactly four writers at HEAD** — the short-rest button (line 7149), `` `function _storyRollInit()@24625` `` at battle start (24650), `` `function storyShortRest(nodeCode)@25818` `` (25841) and `` `function storyConfirmSleep()@36226` `` at +8 (36300) — and **none is fishing**. This is not rot: at `017d7d8`, the commit §TIMELESS-01 was specified against, there were **six** writers (the same four plus movement and the +2 hunt), and none was fishing then either. The hint has been decorative since `213d14b` introduced it. **Second, separable divergence in the same subsystem:** sleeping advances `hoursElapsed` by **8** (36300) but the 24-hour wall clock `S_story.hour` by only **6** (36299), so the two drift apart permanently — and the wall clock is what decides night fishing (`isNight = hour >= 20 || hour <= 5`, §XLVIII), so the night-fish pool's availability slides against elapsed time over a 49-day run. **Design call, three options:** (a) charge the advertised hour in `storyFishing` — restores truth in labelling and gives the doom clock a fourth real cost, but makes fishing measurably more expensive and wants a playtest; (b) drop the `1h` tag and the hint's hour clause — one-line, zero balance impact, and the honest reading of *"only movement is timeless"* was never *"and fishing"*; (c) leave both and document it. **Recommend (b)** — the §TIMELESS-01 design intent was to put the clock's cost on decisions the player chose, and a fishing session that costs nothing is consistent with that; the label is the only thing lying. The sleep/wall-clock split is a separate yes/no: make them agree at 8, or state in a comment that the wall clock deliberately advances less. **Verify:** every `⏱ N` hint on a story card has a matching writer, or no hint; `hour` and `hoursElapsed` agree on sleep or the divergence is commented.

### §CHRON-01 — the career ledger does not survive New Game+ (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02q, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§CHRON-01 (a) — `careerStats` is zeroed by `storyNewGamePlus()`, which is the exact transition the dual ledger was built for.** The Chronicle (§XLII, 2026-05-25) stores two stat objects: `careerStats: { kills:0@23147` and its `runStats` twin, both written by `function _statTally(key, n) {@23916`, which increments both on every one of its fourteen call sites. The **respawn** path is correct — `const _survivingCareerStats@23926` copies the career ledger out, reloads the checkpoint and restores it. The **NG+** path is not: `function storyNewGamePlus() {@24002` preserves six named fields (`npcFavorability`, `pitPerks`, `ngPlusRun`, `entry42Written`, `entry42Text`, `questMinusOne`) and then calls `Object.assign(S_story, _S_DEFAULTS())`. **`careerStats` is not on that list, so it is wiped.** `lab-report-kenickie-chronicle.md` §II-B states the opposite (*"careerStats is preserved across the NG+ initialization… a player on NG+3 who has completed 400 battles total will see 400 in their career ledger"*) and that is the ledger's whole stated rationale — but the same six-name preserve list is already in `cab8865`, the chronicle's own birth commit, so **this has never worked**, not once, in 79 days.
> **The consequence compounds, and it is why nobody has reported it.** `ngPlusRun` **is** preserved, so the game knows it is on run four while the career ledger says nothing has ever happened. And the character sheet blanks the career column when `const isFirstRun = (cs.deaths || 0) === 0;@37710` — so a zeroed career ledger **also hides itself**: an NG+3 player reads a character sheet byte-identical to one who has never died. No error, no empty column, no zero. ***The surface silently agrees with the wipe***, the same shape as §DX-02n's write-only fields but one level up — here the reader exists and renders correctly on data that has been destroyed.
> **Fix:** one line, mirroring `savedFavorability` — save `careerStats` before the `Object.assign` and restore it after. No design call: the report, the field name and the sheet's own `All Lives` heading all specify the same behaviour. **Verify by asserting a post-NG+ `careerStats.kills` is non-zero and that the character sheet renders the second column**, not merely that the field exists.

- [ ] **§CHRON-01 (b) — `sleeps` and `daysAdventuring` are the same number, and both are printed (small writing call).** `_statTally('sleeps', 1);@36279` and `_statTally('daysAdventuring', 1);@36280` are adjacent, unconditional, and are each field's **only** writer, so the two can never diverge — yet `function _populateGameoverChronicle() {@23853` prints both as separate rows (*"Sleeps"*, *"Days adventuring"*) and the character sheet prints both again. The report asserts they *"serve different systems"*; measured, only `S_story.gameDay = (S_story.gameDay || 0) + 1;@36253` does — it has its own readers (town crier, night ambient, Petra's stall, the no-questions vendor cycle) and is legitimately separate. Either give `daysAdventuring` a second writer that earns it (day passage from any source, not only sleep) or drop one row from each of the two surfaces. **Not a bug — a duplicated line item that makes the run summary read as if it measured two things.**

### §DROP-01-FU — the loot migration was reverted three hours after it shipped and only two thirds of it came back (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02v, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DROP-01-FU (a) — `const LOOT_TABLE = [@24442` is still declared, still holds 20 entries, still has zero readers, and its own comment still names `_rollD100Loot()` as its caller.** `lab-report-loot-drop-system-v2.md` §II-B Defect 3 identified it, §IV-A Change 1 deleted it, and `440eb5d` (2026-06-05 10:41) shipped that deletion. `88d41d1` (13:33 the same day) put it back. `0fffce7` (2026-07-07) re-shipped the *other* two changes and left this one. It has since been re-found independently **twice** — §DOC-02b as a zero-reader structure, §DOC-02j as a d20 table whose own comment miscalls it d100 (EV 27.75 vs `_D100_TABLE`'s 21.0). **Fix:** delete the array. Verify with `grep -c LOOT_TABLE play.html` = 0 and no `check:walk` red. Then sync the three docs that already claim it is gone (see §DX-02v).
- [ ] **§DROP-01-FU (b) — the `_D100_TABLE` header comment cannot be fixed in the HTML, because the HTML does not own it.** §V-A row 4 of the same report says the comment was *"updated to document consumables-only schema."* HEAD's comment still reads `{ weight, _type, _magic? }` and still lists `'dagger'|'mainweapon'` — because the block sits inside `◆◆◆ WORLDBUILDER:D100_TABLE ◆◆◆` and the comment is a hard-coded template literal in **`src/js/wbapi-server.js:function serializeD100Table(entries) {@1855`**. HEAD's text is that literal character for character. **Any API write to the loot table re-emits it**, which is why the report's fix did not survive its own afternoon. ***A fix applied to generated output survives exactly until the generator runs.*** **Fix:** edit the serializer's header string, restart the server (`./wbapi-toggle.sh restart`), and re-write the table once so the file picks it up.

### §DROP-02-FU — a deliberate design decision was silently reverted 2 h 48 min after it shipped, and two home docs have certified the reverted state for 68 days (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02w, 🟡 small design call)

- [ ] **§DROP-02-FU — `3f74596` scoped Luck to exactly one call site with a written rationale per removal; `88d41d1` put all six back.** On 2026-06-05 at **10:45**, `3f74596` *"§DROP-02: luck scoped to fishing loot rarity only"* removed `_luckMod()` from six sites, naming each one: d100 combat drops (*"luck doesn't improve monster loot"*), death saves (*"luck is not a survival stat"*), bait-search DC (*"luck doesn't help finding bait"*), bare-hook catch (*"luck doesn't make you a better caster"*), encounter rate (*"luck doesn't reduce how often enemies appear"*), tournament tie-breaker (*"luck doesn't win sporting events"*). Its commit body ends *"That is the only place luck belongs."* At **13:33 the same day**, `88d41d1` — subject ***"POST /api/import/book: documentation + smoke test cleanup"***, body describing only a `TST_SMOKE` node deletion that produces **zero lines** — restored all six verbatim, `+` for `+` against §DROP-02's `-` lines. **Five are live at HEAD**: `Math.max(0, _luckMod())); // Layer 48: Luck bonus@24547`, `let d20 = Math.ceil(Math.random() * 20) + _luckMod();@25897`, `Luck reduces Survival DC@30492`, `bare hook uses LuckMod@30527`, `outcome = _luckMod() > 0 ? 'win'@34112`. The sixth (encounter rate) survived the revert and was deleted nine days later by `85cc43e` with the corridor layer, for unrelated reasons.
> **This is the SAME commit §DOC-02v caught reverting §DROP-01 and §DROP-03 — so `88d41d1` reverted three tracks in one stroke, and §DROP-02 is the only one that never came back.** §DROP-01 returned 68 days later (`0fffce7`); §DROP-03 was rewritten in six (`baeb10e`). Instrument 21's **third** confirmed instance, and the second row filed against this one commit. It is also the strongest argument yet for **§DX-02aa** (make `./api.sh` refuse a write from a stale server): the same tool-driven revert, the same absent commit message, a second design track.
> **The doc half is why nobody noticed, and it is the trap.** `mechanics.md:373` and `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md:361` both state *"Luck's sole mechanical role is improving what you get from a fish catch. All other former uses (bait search DC, d100 combat drops, death saves, encounter rate, tournament tiebreaker) were removed."* Both were **true for 2 h 48 min** and have been wrong for 68 days. Meanwhile `world.md:1180` still lists **all seven** original applications including the deleted corridor term. **Three home docs, three different games, none of them this one.**
> **Fix (recommended default — re-apply the design decision, do NOT repair the docs first):** re-apply §DROP-02's five surviving removals to the HTML, which makes `mechanics.md` and `mechanics-combat.md` true without editing a word of them, then drop the dead corridor row from `world.md:1180` and add the undocumented tournament tie-break if it is kept. ***Editing the docs to match HEAD would launder a reverted design decision into a shipped one*** (§DOC-02v's dating rule). **The design call is only whether to ratify the revert instead** — if Luck is meant to touch death saves and loot, say so once and update all three docs together.

### §AUDIT-03ag — the Luck balance incentive is not expressible in the shipped point-buy, and it inverts at both defaults (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02w, 🟡 design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03ag — `_calcLuck()` is correct and the space it grades is too narrow to grade.** Layer 48 exists to reward balanced ability scores: `function _calcLuck() {@23439` takes the ceiling of the geometric mean, so one dumped stat drags the product down. Character creation is a closed space — `const _CC_COST = [0,1,2,3,4,5,7,9];@38546`, `const CC_BUDGET = 27;@38547`, scores clamped 8–15 — so the question is decidable by enumeration. **All 191,587 legal allocations: Mod −1 → 960 (0.5 %), Mod 0 → 99,828 (52.1 %), Mod +1 → 90,799 (47.4 %), Mod +2 or better → 0.** Restricted to the **12,282** allocations that spend all 27 points: **12,262 (99.84 %) give exactly +1**, and the only 20 exceptions are the twenty permutations of `{15,15,15,8,8,8}` — the single most extreme dump-stat build in the space — landing on Luck 11, **Mod 0**. Creation ceiling is **Luck 13 / +1** (`12/12/12/13/13/13`). **Mod +2** needs level 20 with all seven `_ASI_LEVELS` spent on balance rather than STR; **Mod +3** needs that *plus* both quest-granted WIS points. So across every character a player can build, the geometric mean discriminates by **one point of modifier**, and only against the one build nobody needs a hidden stat to discourage.
> **And the sign inverts at the two no-input paths.** Hard mode (`const scores = _cc_mode === 'hard'` → `{str:10, dex:8, con:8, int:8, wis:8, cha:8}`, the same literal as `abilityScores: { str:10, dex:8, con:8, int:8, wis:8, cha:8 },@23047`) and the untouched custom panel (`let _cc_scores = { str:10, dex:10, con:10, int:8, wis:8, cha:8 };@38549`) **both yield Luck 9, Mod −1** — the worst value in the space: −1 on every death save, +1 on the fishing find DC, and an automatic **loss** on every tournament tie (`outcome = _luckMod() > 0 ? 'win'@34112` reads `< 0 ? 'lose'`). *A hidden reward for balance that hands the least engaged player the worst value is a hidden punishment wearing the reward's clothes.*
> **Three options, design call — do not build before it.** (1) **Widen the output scale** — e.g. `Math.round(_calcLuck() * 1.5 - 5)` or a direct spread-based term, so the 8–15 range maps onto more than one modifier step. (2) **Raise the point-buy cap** past 15 so the geometric mean has room; costs balance work elsewhere. (3) **Accept Luck as flavour** — keep the `✦ LUCK@37670` sheet line as a reading of the six scores and stop wiring mechanics to it, which is close to what §DROP-02 already decided and pairs naturally with §DROP-02-FU.
> **New instrument (22) for the §DOC-02 program:** when a report claims a design constant creates an incentive, **enumerate the space the player chooses inside before believing it**. A formula can be perfectly correct about a gradient the surrounding constants never let the player stand on — here the discriminating power was spent by a cost table ~15,000 lines away, and neither site names the other.

### §DX-02ah — the quest panel renders every active quest at every node, and its own comment says otherwise (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02z, 🟡 design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ah — `// ── QUESTS section (active quests at this node) ────@35527` is followed by a filter that has no node term.** `.filter(([, s]) => s === 'active')@35530` runs over the whole `S_story.quests` map; nothing downstream compares `q.activateNode` to `node.code`. The Ceremonia roll card (`rollBtn.addEventListener('click', () => { _closeCerem(); _rollCeremonia(id); });@35631`) therefore renders — and rolls — for **any** active `skill_check`/`combat`/`delivery`/`escort`/`dialogue` quest, **at any node in the world**. The sibling branch one screen down is node-aware and correct (`const atDest = q.waypointNode && node.code === q.waypointNode;@35651` decides Fight-Now vs Navigate), so this is a divergence *inside one loop*, not a missing convention.
> **It is load-bearing before it is a defect, which is why it needs a call rather than a fix.** §BOARD-01 posts bounties for distant nodes and pre-activates them through `unlock`; `function _bountyPostable(q, node)@37131` requires only `if (!NODE_MAP[q.activateNode]) return false;@37136` — existence, never cell primacy. **The unfiltered panel is the only reason a board-accepted skill-check bounty is completable at all**, and §DOC-02z measured it rescuing §SPARK-01 SEA (3 quests) and keeping `quest_1367_c_ottoman` alive on non-primary `ATH`. Remove the leak and those go dead with it.
> **What it costs on the other side.** §BOARD-01-FU3 pairs each card with a road-weighted leg count (`const legs = _roadGridPath(null, b.destCode).length;@37192`) to answer *"is this trip worth it?"* — for a skill-check bounty the answer is currently **no trip at all**, so the honest-distance label is cosmetic. §BOARD-01-FU2's auto-waypoint has the same problem. And thematically the player rolls the Tilbury Star's bilge investigation while standing in Birka.
> **Three options.** (1) **Ratify it** — declare the panel a global mission log by design, fix the comment, and accept that accepting a bounty is the travel. (2) **Scope the roll to the node, keep the listing global** — render every active quest but gate the roll button on `node.code === q.activateNode`, which preserves the board's discovery value and restores the leg count's meaning; **blocked by §AUDIT-03x**, because a bounty at a non-primary node would then be unrollable everywhere. (3) **Scope both** — cleanest, and it silently kills every quest §AUDIT-03x has stranded. **Sequence after §DX-02w / §AUDIT-03x**; option 2 is the recommended default once primacy is fixed.

### §DX-02ai — two naval-layer beats pay twice and announce once (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02z, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ai — a `storyRender` button that writes a quest's completion flag, when the quest also carries a reward, pays both.** Two live instances, both on **reachable** nodes, both player-facing today:
>   - **`quest_sk_hull` (Saltwick dry dock, `MME`) — 400 XP paid, 200 announced.** The button pays its price correctly through the `cost` leaf (`kind:'cost', gold:200@33906`), then grants `S_story.xp = (S_story.xp||0) + 200;@33910` inline and toasts *"-200gp. +200 XP."* Its own `storyRender(node)` re-entry completes the quest (`completion:{ flags:['shipRepaired'] }`), whose `onComplete` carries a second `{ kind:'reward', xp:200 }`.
>   - **`quest_spark2_05` (Fehn confrontation, `DNF`) — 1,000 XP paid, 400 announced.** The button grants +400 gold and +400 XP, sets `fehnConfessed`, and the quest then pays `xpAward:600` through `if (q.type === 'side' && q.xpAward) { S_story.xp += q.xpAward; _checkLevelUp(); }@30203`.
> **The other nine button/quest pairs in the layer are clean** — payment sits on exactly one side each — so this is two sites, not a pattern in the arc's authoring. Same class as the four fixed by `3338def` (§SPARK-01-FU / §LXX-01-FU); these survived because that sweep was scoped to the harbour and §LXX stacks.
> **Fix:** delete the inline grant and let the quest pay (matching the nine clean pairs), or drop the quest-side reward. **Prefer deleting the inline grant** — the toast text then agrees with the ledger without being edited, and §VM-01-G4a's whole argument is that value should move through the VM. **Verify with a Playwright pin on `S_story.xp` delta across one click**, not on the toast string.
> **Detector wanted (mechanical):** for every `S_story.<flag> = true` inside a `storyRender` block, find the quest whose `completion.flags` names that flag and flag the pair if **both** carry a payout. That is a static scan over two registries the repo already parses.

### §DX-02aq — the Necklace of Knowledge is a minority writer into its own array, and the other fourteen render as `undefined` (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02af, 🟡 no design call on the fix, a small one on the surface)

- [ ] **§DX-02aq — `S_story.knowledge` carries two incompatible value shapes and the renderer tests neither.** Layer 13's Necklace shipped exactly as its spec wrote it: `function _maybeAddKnowledgeBead@25809` pushes `{ name, icon, node, type:'knowledge' }`, byte-exact to `lab-report-prompt-migration-arena-to-prototype.md` and to `lab-report-drop-rates-balance-and-health.md` (§DOC-02j verified the same record independently). **It is now 1 of 15 push sites.** The other fourteen push a **bare string** — investigation lore notes from the Warmth arc (`The Warmth: a colonial microorganism…`), the Deep Warmth Eel, the Relay Road, the Lake, the Dunfall Drift Spore, the Bilge Mystery, Roen's Personal Legend, Ardley's Laws ×2, the Cycle-4 archive note, the Atlantean Foundation note and the Crone Bead ×2 — plus `st.knowledge.push(bit.knowledge)@22277`, the UQF `reward` bit's clue field, which forwards whatever a quest author wrote.
> **The defect is player-visible in the panel whose whole purpose is legibility.** The inventory renderer at `S_story.knowledge.forEach(bead@31233` builds each row as `bead.icon` + `bead.name` + a `memory` tag with **no type test**. On a string primitive both properties are `undefined`, so **every lore note renders under 🔮 Necklace of Knowledge as the literal row `undefinedundefined memory`** — one row per note. A player who has run any of those arcs sees a necklace of `undefined`s beside their real beads.
> **No gate can see it, and the reason is structural:** this is a *field-shape* contract, not a node, npc or quest reference. `check:noderegs` / `check:npcregs` / `check:dupkeys` / `check:questgraph` are all blind to it, and so is §DX-02n's proposed `check:deadconsts` — the field has plenty of readers and plenty of writers; what it lacks is agreement between them. It is the **inverse** of the §DX-02n family: not a structure with zero readers, but **one reader serving two incompatible writer shapes**.
> **Both designs are documented, independently, and neither knows about the other.** The bead half is specified in two 2026-05-21 lab reports; the clue-store half is this file's own mystery-arc row, which cites `S_story.knowledge[]` as an existing cumulative-clue store and the same `.inv-item-knowledge@1546` / `.inv-item-knowledge@3238` CSS as its render path. **Fix (no design call):** normalise on write — wrap a string push as `{ name: <the text>, icon:'📖', type:'lore' }`, or normalise on read with a `typeof bead === 'string'` branch in the renderer. **Small design call on the surface only:** whether lore notes belong in the Necklace section at all, or in their own 📖 Field Notes section beneath it — they are a different kind of memory (what you learned vs. where you slept), and the report's own thesis is that a bead means *"I was here"*. Recommend a separate section plus a normalising writer, so the Necklace stays the geography it was designed to be. **Verify:** a test asserting every element of `S_story.knowledge` has a string `name` and a string `icon` after each of the fifteen push sites, and that the rendered panel contains no `undefined`.

### §DX-02ac — eight dead `||` fallback literals name a starting character the game has never made, and four documents believed them (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02w, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ac — `const s = S_story.abilityScores || { str:16, dex:12, con:14, int:10, wis:12, cha:8 };@23440` and seven siblings.** The `||` branch is unreachable: `abilityScores: { str:10, dex:8, con:8, int:8, wis:8, cha:8 },@23047` is declared in `_S_DEFAULTS()` and `const scores = startScores || { str:10, dex:8, con:8, int:8, wis:8, cha:8 };@23953` re-asserts it on every new game, so `abilityScores` is never absent. The `16/12/14/10/12/8` statline **has never been a starting character** — not at HEAD, not at `0a131f5`, not at any point measured. `grep -c` finds it **8 times** across the file as a `||` fallback.
> **The cost is not dead weight, it is four wrong documents.** `lab-report-luck-seventh-stat.md` §II-A read the fallback as the default and built its flagship worked example on it (*"Starting Human Fighter (STR 16, DEX 12, CON 14, INT 10, WIS 12, CHA 8) … Luck 12, Mod +1"*; the real default is **Luck 9, Mod −1**). That example propagated into `lab-report-fishing-bait-prompting.md`'s Appendix B and into **`mechanics.md:356`** and **`docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md:344`**, all four printing *"Default scores (STR:16, DEX:12, CON:14…)"*. ***A write-only field is inert; a dead `||` branch that names a plausible data shape reads exactly like a declaration.*** Sixth widening of the proposed `check:deadconsts` — after top-level consts, quest-entry fields, parameters, item objects and `||`-chain alternatives (§DX-02n (e)), this is the first where the alternative is a **plausible-looking literal** rather than an identifier, so a reader/writer census cannot even see the question.
> **Fix:** collapse all eight to `S_story.abilityScores` (or to a single named `const _DEFAULT_SCORES` that equals `_S_DEFAULTS()`'s value), then correct the four documents. Verify with `grep -c "str:16, dex:12, con:14"` = 0.
> **EXTENDED 2026-08-13 (§DOC-02be) — a FIFTH document, and it is a synthesis, which is how the class spreads.** `lab-report-synthesis-part4-monsters-fishing.md` carried *"Starting scores (STR 16, DEX 12, CON 14, INT 10, WIS 12, CHA 8) yield Luck 12, Mod +1"* — inherited from `lab-report-fishing-bait-prompting.md` Appendix B, not re-derived. The real default `(10,8,8,8,8,8)` gives product 327,680 → **Luck 9, Mod −1**, so the advice the four documents build on this example (*invest broadly and the lake pays*) is being illustrated with a character whose Luck is a **penalty**. **The row's cost estimate should now read five documents, and the mechanism is worth naming:** a cross-reference document copies its predecessors' *conclusions* verbatim while re-checking only their *pointers*, so a wrong literal propagates at the same rate as a right one and arrives looking corroborated. Fixing the eight `||` branches does not fix this — **the four (now five) documents need editing by hand**, and the verify grep must be run over `docs/lab-reports/` and `docs/` as well as the HTML.

### §DX-02ae — a render getter writes a progression flag, and a scene comment disagrees with its own guard (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02y, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ae (a) — `ACT8_FAREWELL_BEATS.brynn.text` is a `text: () => {…}` accessor that MUTATES `S_story`.** Rendering the Act VIII Brynn farewell runs `lampLine = BRYNN_KEEPER_STORY.farewellNoStory;@26894` and, on the next line, `S_story.brynnKeeperStoryTold = true`. The write is **correct as design** — `farewellNoStory` is a catch-up delivery (*"There's a lamp in the corner that's been burning since your first night. I wanted you to know that."*), so the player who never found Beat 1 does now know, and the epilogue row keyed on that flag reads truthfully. **The hazard is the placement, not the write.** Two downstream consumers read it: the Town Crier's `qOrder = @26866` (which is where the lamp news actually ships, under `brynnKeeperStoryTold:      'The lamp at the First Inn@26776`) and `_buildEpilogueScroll`. It is safe today **only because `_renderNpcCard` sets its `act8Farewell<Key>` latch BEFORE invoking `beat.text()`** — remove that ordering, add a preview render, or call the getter twice for any reason, and the branch silently advances from `farewellNoStory` to `farewellNoChoice` with no error. **Fix:** move the flag write to the call site, beside the latch, and leave `text()` pure. ***A render accessor that mutates progression state is one refactor away from a double-fire, and nothing in the type system or the gates can see the coupling.***
- [ ] **§DX-02ae (b) — `const YAEL_NAMED_REPORT_SCENE = {@28049`'s own `// → doc:` comment says *"fav >= 2 Act IV+"* and the guard says Act VI.** `if (node.code === 'LHR' && _npcFavor('yael') >= 2@32471` is followed by `(S_story.actNumber || 1) >= 6`. One line apart, in the same file, and the annotation is the half a doc reader trusts. One-word fix; recorded because the `// → doc:` corpus is 93 annotations and nothing audits their *contents* (§DOC-02x).

### §DX-02bt — two quest-panel renderers still concatenate save-file and world-data strings into `innerHTML` (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02ba, 🟡)

- [ ] **§DX-02bt — §DATA-01's own §2 thesis, finished for one renderer and not for its two siblings.** The 2026-07-06 residual fix converted the main quest-panel renderer to DOM construction (`titleRow.className = 'quest-title'@30770`, `textContent` for title/desc/hint). **Two sibling renderers in the same function were left on string-concatenated `innerHTML`, and both interpolate data-originated strings rather than code-generated ones:** (1) `<span>Hunt: '@30806` interpolates `customWp.label` twice plus `dest.label` read from `NODE_MAP` — node labels are authorable through the WBAPI worldbuilder; (2) `<span>🦴 Your body at '@30825` interpolates `q.nodeName` from `S_story.corpsesQuests`, which is **persisted in the save file and read back on load**, i.e. from further outside the bundle than `QUEST_DB` ever was.
> **Current exposure is zero and that is the point:** re-audited at HEAD, **0 `label:` and 0 `title:` values in `play.html` contain `<`**. The original report's argument applies verbatim — *"the guarantee should not depend on the current content of the data."* **Fix:** the same `document.createElement` + `textContent` treatment already applied at 30769; the badge/button markup stays on `insertAdjacentHTML` because it is code-generated. **Sub-item (🟢, same file, unrelated hazard):** `const NPC_DIALOGUES = {@10396` and `const NPC_DIALOGUE = {@22445` are two distinct live objects one character apart (5 and 9 readers respectively; `storyShowNpc` reads the singular). Rename or comment the pair before the next author picks the wrong one.

### §VM-01 — The Quest VM → *No Word for Wait* (NEW 2026-07-16)

> **Diagnostic (parent) lab report:** `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-javascript-mud.md` — a structural read of the entire `<script>` block (five layers, four execution traces: program/UI/movement/quest-acceptance), plus the follow-up verification recorded below. **Not** a quest-by-quest audit. **Inc A child lab report LOCKED 2026-07-21:** `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-vm01a-execbits-coroutine.md` (design-review-before-implementation; locks the `execBits`→generator transformation, the `ask` envelope, the driver, the five call-site wraps, the three design calls, and the test plan — see §RESUME NEWEST banner above). Policy: **Host/Script Separation Policy** in [CONTRIBUTING.md](../../CONTRIBUTING.md).
>
> **The unifying theme (matched to `story.md`): the engine tells, and cannot ask.** `story.md` is about a man who saw the Void and never managed to *ask anyone for help* — Froberger fixed it alone and it destroyed him. The quest engine commits the same act on the player: **you are told (`📋 <title>`), never asked.** Arrival + gate = `'active'`, announced in a `·`-joined message strip. There is no accept button — and the reason is not that nobody added one. **There is no state in which the VM is waiting for an answer.** (§PLAY-01 found the engine *knows what it won't transmit*; §VM-01 finds it **cannot receive**. Same sin, opposite direction.)
>
> **The architecture is already right — this track finishes §ARCH-01, it does not redesign it.** The file runs a proper embedded-scripting split twice over: three pure, world-injected, parity-fenced kernels (`MOVER`/`ROOMS`/`DUEL`, byte-identical to `src/js/*.js` with CI assertions), and a 258-line `QuestRuntime` executing 11,106 lines of `QUEST_DB` through the `BIT_CONTRACTS` opcode table. That 43:1 ratio is the achievement. **The defect is one small thing on top of it.**
>
> **Root cause — the VM has twelve opcodes and no jump instruction.** `execBits` (`execBits(bits, ctx)@22224`) in full: a `for` loop that looks up `HANDLERS[bit.kind]`, calls `h(bit, ctx)`, and ignores the return. No branch. No suspend. Three verified proofs, all grepped this session, none recalled:
>
> 1. **`choice` has a complete contract and an empty handler.** The contract (`choice:      { required@21998`) is rigorous — requires `prompt` + `options`, validates ≥2 options each carrying a `label` and a `bits` array. The implementation (`choice(bit@22320`) is, in full: `choice(bit, ctx) { /* Phase 2: renderChoiceBlock(bit.prompt, bit.options, ctx) */ }`. **Zero quests use it** (`grep -c "kind:'choice'"` → 0). It was never written because **a synchronous `for` loop cannot wait for a human being.**
> 2. **`item_check` writes a predicate nobody reads.** `ctx._itemCheck@22312` sets it; a full-file grep returns that line and nothing else. It is a conditional in a language with no `if` — it evaluates and evaporates.
> 3. **`skill_check` works only by smuggling.** It is the one bit that branches, and it does so by hardcoding the branch *inside the leaf*: `resolveSkillCheck` → `execBits(pass ? bit.onPass : bit.onFail)` (`execBits(pass ?@22260`). The sole conditional in the whole language is baked into one instruction rather than available to it.
>
> **Two missing words, and everything follows.** `wait` (no `yield` → no consent, no `choice`, no dialogue tree, no multi-step interaction, no `'abandoned'` status, no transactional bit chain — a throw mid-chain leaves half the effects applied with no rollback). `or` (no boolean nesting → each new OR shape has historically meant a new *term*; `itemsMinAny` was added for exactly one quest, `quest_wm_01`). **Prior art worth reading before Inc A:** Ink (Inkle) compiles narrative to JSON bytecode on a small VM and suspends at a `ChoicePoint` — structurally what this is; Yarn Spinner for dialogue; CEL / JSONLogic for Inc F (expression-AST-as-data, no parser needed).
>
> **Per Lab Report Policy: every increment locks its own child lab report (data shapes + flow) before any HTML edit.** The rows below are the *plan of a plan* — scope, evidence, dependency, and the decision each one carries. They are not implementation plans and must not be treated as one.
>
> **Dependency order:** A · B · C are independent (A is the keystone). D needs C. E needs C + the `_legacy_fn` purge. F is independent but cheaper after C. G needs A + F to be worth doing.

> *Closed increments A–F + F-FU (A/B/C `c22f4f0` · D `9f10bfe` · E `354b20a` · F `c6be7f8` · F-FU `549d6b4`) — full ship records moved verbatim → plan-archive.md §"Archived 2026-07-28 — §DX-01f". Only §VM-01-G below remains open.*

- [~] **§VM-01-G — *The migration front*: per-node special cases → node hooks — DESIGN LOCKED 2026-07-28 (`docs/lab-reports/lab-report-vm01g-migration-front.md`): user chose Option C (layered) via Ask — NODE_PANELS ordered data table for the ~30 Class-A/B flavor/arrival blocks · NODE_HOOKS registered render fns for the ~9 Class-E bespoke UIs · Class C/D migrate INTO the VM per-arc (real `gate:`+`activateNode`; `choice` + a new `cost` leaf — Inc A's first content consumer); slice order G1→G2→G3→G4 confirmed; G4 OK in principle but needs its own child design pass first. Re-measure at lock: front at 33332, `node.code` comparisons 76→124 (77 above the front across ~58 blocks + 47 engine-region Class-F, logged as G-FU) — the front moves backward ~4/day, the row's thesis is measurable, not theoretical.** Original finding: `storyRender` (now `30887`–`35298`, **4,412 lines — the largest function in the file**) is two programs stacked: ~2,445 lines of hardcoded per-node special cases followed by a compact, generic, data-driven `_mkSection`/`_mkCard` engine that renders Fish/Encounter/Quests/Loot/Rest/World from data. Every `if (node.code === 'XX')` above the front is a missing component — `isFishingLake` is what one looks like when it went right. This is plain ECS, **the same disease as the gate language one layer up:** *when the data language lacks a shape, the file grows imperative code instead of vocabulary.* Two hazards the report names as load-bearing: **insertion order is LIFO** (every panel block inserts `afterend` of `story-text-box`, so later blocks render ABOVE earlier — the table must be order-preserving by construction) and **remove-then-recreate semantics** (render is re-entrant; remove managed ids first, then create the active set). The §PLAY-01-G lesson applies directly — **`birkaNpcs` had five NPC cards keyed to dead pre-§WALK node codes and rendered nowhere for months**; special-case blocks keyed on `node.code` rot silently. Slices: **G1** NODE_PANELS (~700–900 lines → one table + ~40-line renderer; provable no-op via golden-DOM parity) · **G2** NODE_HOOKS (verbatim extraction, golden-DOM parity) · **G3** Class C per-arc (WM/cat/VS/NG+ CI → real gates; arc tests + `check:questgraph`) · **G4** Class D per-verb (child report first) · **G-FU** the 47 engine-region exceptions. **⟶ G1 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 (`25c3710` + fixes `bf6f486`/`7a130cd`):** 12 order-safe Class-A/B panels (CI tagline · AO name-change · KS conversion · LT stoning · §SIREN teaser · LCA close · Malta snake · Sweelinck variant · Quest −1 door · IST/TBS/MCT approaches) migrated into the ordered `NODE_PANELS` table + one `_renderNodePanels` loop at the stale-sibling sweep; once-flags (`saulConverted`/`maltaSnakeEvent`) via the table's `once` field; LIFO stacking replicated by construction (table order = old source order, all inserts afterend of `#story-text-box`). **Proof: 24-combo golden-DOM before/after diff = byte-identical** (DOM + flag writes; temp harness deleted after the proof), `uqf-node-panels.test.js` 7/7 permanent regression, `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303**, `npc-card-map` 23/23, all `check:walk` sub-checks green (QUEST:CORE 22519 bytes identical), only J14/J15 + TGS/SPB baseline reds. **Implementation refinements over the lab report (grep-before-building):** (1) the report's ~22-block Class-A count included the Birka-region sub-blocks, which anchor to `npcRowDiv` not `story-text-box` — a different anchor class, excluded from G1 (candidates for a second table after G2); (2) order-safety excludes the BK approach panel (Corelli's button precedes it and can co-fire) and the NUE/TLS mid-sequence reminders (Prior Carrier/Inquisitor/Codex-Core) — these stay inline until G3/G4 migrate the conflicting Class-C/D neighbors, which dissolves the constraint. **G1 findings — two DEAD panels surfaced and fixed as one-line follow-ups:** the §SIREN-01 navigator teaser was keyed to `LJ3`, a node code that was NEVER BUILT (maps.md documents LJ0–LJ3 sea-battle junctions; only the courts shipped) → remapped to LSO, `quest_sea_overseer`'s activateNode (`bf6f486`); the §LXII AO name-change notice used `!visited['HTY']` as its once-guard, but `storyCollectLoot` flips `visited[code]` earlier in the same render, dead since it shipped → dedicated `aoNameNoticeSeen` once-flag (`7a130cd`). Both now render for the first time ever — the row's silent-rot thesis, proven twice more by the migration itself. Docs synced: story.md §SIREN injections + maps.md junction rows flagged ⚠ NOT AS-BUILT (the section's plan topology/coords diverge from NODE_MAP — a candidate for a future maps.md sync pass). **⟶ G2 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28: NODE_HOOKS — 7 Class-E bespoke blocks (~440 lines) leave storyRender as verbatim registered hooks, proven byte-identical.** Extracted: Void Archaeology (Layer 52, 5-node investigate UI) · Void Shaman Warden (Layer 56) · Corelli Merchant (Layer 61) · Codex Core Chamber (§D01-10) · La Riva row (Layer 78) · Scholar Workshop (Layer 81) · Mimic Meadows (Layer 81) — each hook body is the VERBATIM former block, still self-gating. **One design deviation, recorded in the lab report §8:** the sketched "single dispatch loop" is order-UNSAFE — Corelli's gate is the computed `_checkCorelliAppearance` schedule (any of 5 nodes), so no single dispatch position avoids crossing a same-node co-firing block, and a keyed `NODE_HOOKS[code]` map can't even express that gate. Shipped shape = ordered `NODE_HOOKS` registry (`nodes` = tooling metadata, `null` for computed schedules) + **in-place dispatch**: `_runNodeHook(id, node)` sits at the exact source position each block occupied, so LIFO stacking is preserved **by construction** — the G1 order-safety rule needs no per-block exclusion analysis, and G2b/G3 candidates aren't order-blocked. **Proof: 14-combo golden-DOM before/after diff byte-identical** (story-column HTML + move-msg + quest/flag probes, 0 page errors both sides; temp harness deleted); new `uqf-node-hooks.test.js` **4/4** (registry integrity · one live surface probe per hook · LIFO pin vs the inline ZRH Secret Gate · source guard: no block body remains inline); regression `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303** + `uqf-node-panels` 7/7 + `npc-card-map` 22/22 (332/332 in one run); all four parity fences byte-identical (`MOVER` 1847 / `ROOMS` 10836 / `QUEST` 22519 / `DUEL` 7987), `check:gateast` 76 / `check:rng` / `check:questgraph` green; only the documented J14/J15 + TGS/SPB baseline reds. Side-finding: fresh SZG legitimately renders no workshop panel (the node's own `loot` auto-grants the Prototype Wand on arrival, gating the wand button off) — test probe adjusted, not a regression. **Deferred to G2b:** the Birka-region nested UIs (Blue Shutters · Froberger Memorial · Pachelbel/Couperin ledgers · Pit Championship · Kenickie shop · Kern & Sable) — `npcRowDiv` anchor / Layer-41 scope, need a ctx-argument design; CDG/Tilbury/Visby mixed blocks are G3's territory. Hazard #1 respected (`:1367` verified stopped). **Next slice: G3 (Class C per-arc quest activation → real gates), or G2b.** `storyRender` (`30502`–`34862`, **4,360 lines — the largest function in the file**) is two programs stacked: ~2,400 lines of hardcoded per-node special cases (76 `node.code === '…'` comparisons across ~40 blocks; Birka NPC cards alone are `31810`–`32917`) followed at `32918` by a compact, generic, data-driven `_mkSection`/`_mkCard` engine that renders Fish/Encounter/Quests/Loot/Rest/World from data. **The migration front is a horizontal line at 32918 and the open question is whether it's still moving.** Every `if (node.code === 'XX')` above it is a missing component — `isFishingLake` is what one looks like when it went right (add the flag in `NODE_MAP`, the node gets a fishing card, ~8 lines). This is plain ECS. **It is the same disease as the gate language one layer up:** *when the data language lacks a shape, the file grows imperative code instead of vocabulary.* **Which is why it is last** — fix the vocabulary in A and F first and a real fraction of the 2,400 lines becomes expressible as data rather than needing a bespoke hook. **Lock first:** whether node hooks are `[bits]` (reuse the VM — attractive once A lands) or registered render fns; and the §PLAY-01-G lesson applies directly — **`birkaNpcs` had five NPC cards keyed to dead pre-§WALK node codes and rendered nowhere for months.** Special-case blocks keyed on `node.code` rot silently. That is the argument for this increment, and it is not theoretical. **G3 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — Class C is done, and the silent-rot thesis proved out three more times over** (full record: lab report §9). The 5 activation blocks (NG+/WM/TL/VS/cat — 15 quests + vs_02/vs_03/vs_warden/la_riva_02/03 adjacent) became real `gate:`+`activateNode` data with two new host-understood fields — **`onActivate`** (absent → `📋 title` · null → silent · `{msg,delayMs}` → the arcs' bespoke staged narrations, verbatim) and **`boardExempt`** (NG+ remembrance never posts) — and the activation loop was extracted to `_uqfActivateAtNode`, run at storyRender START so per-node UI keyed on `'active'` sees same-arrival activations (the Solvak-button lesson). **Zero new gate leaves needed** (`countMin` on `ngPlusRun`, `questsDone`, `notFlags` covered everything — the "act ≥ N" legs were structurally dead: `actNumber` = `node.act`, constant per node). **What grep-before-building found:** (1) audit waves `a721254`/`ea02faf` had APPENDED duplicate `activateNode` keys below the authored `activateNode:null` — last-key-wins had the whole cat chain + tl_02/03 + vs_02/03/warden + la_riva_02/03 mass-activating with vacuous gates, so the legacy stanzas' staging was already dead; (2) `710bb75`'s SF→LCY "fix" was a wrong-target dead-code remap (SF = Storefront = STN) — tl_01/tl_03 pointed at the wrong city; (3) **`NODE_MAP.VS` and `NODE_MAP.TL` had no `code` field — the entire VS arc and the TL Vonn path had NEVER run in live play**; fixed via `./api.sh put node` (+ two latent double-pay bugs in the revived Ori/seal buttons fixed by paying through the quests' own onComplete chains, keyed on quest-active). Board consequence (deliberate): `_bountyPostable`'s "gate satisfied ⇒ in-sequence" promise is now TRUE — the slate sheds exactly the out-of-sequence chapters; FU6 legitimacy tests updated to in-sequence-at-referral-time; Yva's button re-keyed on `quest_vs_02` active heals the referral dead-end. **Evidence:** 20-combo pre/post golden-state diff **14 byte-identical · 6 intended · 0 unexpected**; new `uqf-quest-activation.test.js` **8/8**; `quest-runtime-uqf` 303/303, warrants-board 25/25, npc-card-map 22/22, panels 7 + hooks 4 + gate-ast/coroutine/env/softlock/quest-core + 4 smokes (410 total in final runs); all four parity fences byte-identical (QUEST:CORE untouched); `check:walk` green except the documented J14/J15 + TGS/SPB baselines; §7½ eyeballed (VS: Solvak card + button + listed quest; CDG: staged). So `_runNodeHook(id, node, ctx)` forwards `{ npcRowDiv }`, the **21** hooks that append destructure it in the signature and the **8** that write no DOM here take `(node)` alone and are asserted never to name the anchor. It needed a mechanism the VM lacked — **chain failure** via `ctx._halt`, which `execBits` breaks on and **deliberately never clears**, because `ctx` is shared with the nested `execBits` a `choice` option runs: clearing it would let the bits after an unpaid branch run on. A **D2 exclusive choice has no button**: the options *are* the surface, so giving Kern & Sable a `label` would have inserted a click the player never had to make. The mode is therefore decided by what the entry carries — **`label`+`bits`** = a button that runs on click · **`bits` with no `label`** = the chain IS the surface, runs at render, and its first bit **must** park on a `choice` · **`ambient`** = a flavour line for a state with nothing to do. Running a chain at render is exactly how side effects land on every draw of a node, so a label-less non-suspending chain is **refused with a warning — and refused BEFORE its mount exists.** The first implementation refused *after* inserting it and the test caught that: **a refusal that leaves an empty div behind still changed the page.** **Two behaviour deltas, named rather than discovered:** the driver re-renders when a chain ends, so the Q-NEXUS-01 follow-up appears **in the same beat** as [Ask] instead of on the next arrival (the fiction supports it — they are still at the counter, waiting — and it is asserted, not left to be noticed); and the **empty 8px spacer div** the inline block inserted in the "listened and walked away" state is gone. **Evidence, in the order it was built:** (1) a **multiset diff of the authored strings**, old block vs new table — **14 vs 14, identical, nothing added or lost** (the G2b line-multiset method adapted to a block that *could not* move verbatim: its shape changed, so only its content can be pinned, and pinning the content is what proves no prose was retyped); (2) a **golden-DOM diff of all four DUS states** against HEAD — text and button labels **byte-identical including the 6px/4px spacing**, since `_uqfRenderAsk` was given the first-button 6px every hand-written panel used, leaving only the mount's `id`, a `data-uqf-option` attribute, one redundant wrapper `<div>`+`<em>` dropped from an already-italic `.npc-ambient`, and delta 2; (3) **`uqf-node-verbs.test.js` 10/10 with all 10 failing at HEAD** — **not** G2b's "some pass both ways" shape, and the difference is honest: the surface's DOM identity changed with the migration, so equivalence lives in the golden diff, not in the after-state contract. > **⚠ INSTRUMENT 161 — A COUNT IS ONLY AS WIDE AS ITS QUOTING CONVENTION, AND THIS IS THE ORIGIN OF THE FALSE PREMISE cu FOUND IN FU7.** All **fourteen** of §4's type figures reproduce **to the digit** as `grep -o "type:'X'" | wc -l` — and all fourteen measured half a corpus that is written in two spellings: `type:'skill_check',` **106** *plus* `type:"skill_check",` **2,378** = the real parser's **2,484**. So the board-eligible pool at the pinned parent is **2,760 quests across six types**, not the 249 across three §4 reasons about; the four types §4 calls *"0 today; future-proofed"* hold **164 quests** (`combat` 78 · `delivery` 57 · `escort` 22 · `dialogue` 7); `craft` — which §4 counts at 12 and puts in the allowlist — has **0** top-level `QUEST_DB` entries at the parent **and at HEAD**; and `hybrid` (**13**) is a real type the allowlist excludes and no report in the family has ever named. ***When a corpus has been machine-imported, a hand-written grep measures the hand-written half.*** Use `src/js/wbapi-core.js`.

The first works — proved in Chromium at ship AND at HEAD, identical figures: **4 · 5 · 6 · 6 · 7** cards with the header reading `Unknown (0)` → `Warrant's Own (20)` exactly as §8 promised. **The second is correct code with no reach.** `if (_boardRewardXp(q) > tier.rewardCap) continue@37174` removes **5** candidates from a pool of **1,076** (0.46%); those five sort to positions **265, 461, 828, 859, 887**; the player reads `const shown = out.slice(0, limit@37185` — at most **7**. **Swept over sixty consecutive game days at maximum standing: ZERO days show a premium bounty**, 6 show any reward-xp at all, and **every card on the measured slate at all five ranks scores `_boardRewardXp` = 0** with an empty `rewardStr`. Not a coding bug — a **population** mismatch: the gate acts on the eligible set, the experience is the shown set, and the sort key `(daySeed ^ _boardSeed(q.id))` has never heard of standing, so widening the slice never reorders it. ***A filter and a slice are two different populations: measure a listing gate against what is SHOWN, never against what is eligible.*** **The tell was written down in the report itself** — §9's ceiling test says *"inspect the CANDIDATE POOL (huge limit so the slice cannot hide it)"*. Even the cross-file pair is exact: the server QUEST_DB validation really does span `src/js/wbapi-server.js:4425`–`4846` at the parent. **Instrument 148 (mild but useful): an anchor block measured minutes after a commit lands is offset-zero evidence, and the tell is in the header — *"re-grepped live this session"* plus a ship commit within the hour.**

Method surface unchanged, all six members. **Tests re-run:** `uqf-quest-core` **7/7** (2.6 s — the plan asked for five assertions and the shipped suite adds two), `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303** (3.8 min), `check:questgraph` ✅ **2,853 analysed / 2,804 reachable / 0 residual nondeterminism**, `check:gateast` ✅ 76 assertions. **And the two reds §5 called permanent are both GREEN at HEAD** — `check:invariants` and `check:roads` — because §JUNK-01/§DX-01a removed the J14/J15 junctions outright six days later. A baseline red is as perishable as a baseline green.
>
> **Nine deltas, seven of them framing prose (instrument 9, yet again).** (1) *"2,850 quests"* — the parser says **2,853** at parent, ship **and** HEAD, so it was wrong the day it was typed, and it is the **third sighting**: §VM-01-A and §VM-01-C carry the identical figure, one decoration inherited down the sibling set. (2) *"258-line engine"* — the region its **own anchors bracket** (`21706`–`21965`) is **260** lines, 249 non-blank. (3) *"gates ×~30"* for `S_story` — **40** in the two gates, 50 in the whole literal. (4) *"`check:quest` in `npm run check:walk`"* — it shipped as **`check:questparity`**, and a nonexistent npm script exits 1 and reads exactly like a failing gate.
>
> **§DX-02dv RESOLVED BY MEASUREMENT, and the answer is the kinder one.** That row posed an either/or: is the ship record `286/17` mis-transcribed, or did D genuinely run green at 303? Counted the cases in the test source at four blobs — `c22f4f0`, `9f10bfe`, `bd951d7`, HEAD — **303 at every one**. The report says **286 passed / 17 env-quirk failures**, and 286 + 17 = 303. **The report is the correct witness; `plan-archive.md` dropped the 17.** Instrument **147** then lands on top of it: `bd951d7` retired that very baseline **7 h 28 min** after D shipped — *"17 stale tests, not server clobber (17→1)"*. So the ceremony was sound, the verdict was right (D was a genuine no-op), and the word **baseline** was the fiction. One working day holds the entire A/B/C/D landing and the audit that dissolves the thing all four of them reason through.
>
> **Two code deltas, and both are filed.** **§DX-02dw** 🟢 — §4 design call 2 says *"guards live in the host thunks, not the kernel"*, and **twelve of thirteen** effect call sites honour it (`E.getQuest ? …`, `if (E.checkLevelUp)`, `if (E.mint)`, `if (E.preBattle)`, and `mission_bit(bit, ctx)@22304` even carries an env fallback). The thirteenth is `` `const d20  = Math.ceil(E.rng() * 20)@22249` ``, called bare — **and §4 promises a `Math.random()` fallback that was NEVER SHIPPED**: the kernel holds **zero** `Math.random()` at ship and at HEAD. The zero is better than advertised; the missing guard is not, and it is live — `src/scripts/check-gate-parity.js:const rt = Q.createQuestRuntime@32` is a shipped, green, in-`check:walk` consumer that injects **no** `rng` and survives only because gate evaluation never reaches `_rollSkill`. **§DX-02dx** 🟢 — §6 named §VM-01-E's static gate walker as a likely first consumer; `src/scripts/check-questgraph.js:const Q = require@63` imports the kernel and **`Q` occurs exactly once in the file, on that line**. The walker re-implements what it needs against its own brace matcher. The **real** first consumer is §VM-01-F's gate-parity checker — the substrate paid off one increment later and through a door that was not on the list. **Instrument 149: a `require` is not a consumer. When a track credits an increment with consuming a new module, count the USES of the imported binding, not the import.** Also recorded as an open debt rather than a defect: `src/js/wbapi-server.js` still does not `require('./quest')` 30 days on, so D's headline payoff remains unredeemed — exactly as the report honestly predicted of itself.
>
> **The playability restatement, because it is the point.** A quest VM is invisible and nobody praises a dispatcher. What D buys is downstream: a quest that cannot be finished is the **one bug a player cannot distinguish from their own mistake** — they re-read the hint, re-walk the node, re-load the save, and it was never completable. Across **2,853 quests / 416 nodes** no hand play-test finds those. Making the engine requireable is what turns *"is quest X reachable?"* from *open the game and try* into a gate that runs on every commit, and the parity fence is what stops the checked module and the shipped HTML from drifting into certifying the wrong engine.
>

The spec fused two objects' vocabularies into a name belonging to neither, and **the implementer silently used the correct field** — caught by a person, not by a gate. Not filed: nothing to fix in code.
>
> **AND THIS INCREMENT CREATED TWO OF §DX-02g'S FOUR SILENT-FALLBACK READERS (instrument 7 — corroborated, not re-filed).** §1 states `tier ∈ {easy, medium, hard, deadly}`; the parent's real distribution was **trivial 32 · easy 60 · medium 148 · hard 123 · deadly 33 · rare 1 · low 1**. The implementer caught `trivial` (both helpers ship a row the spec never mentioned) and nobody caught `rare`/`low`, so `_voidEnrage` answers an unknown tier with the **weakest** press and `_fleeChance` with `0.4`. Measured consequence: `void_shaman` — *"The Warden"*, the one monster in the pool whose key literally says Void — shipped `tier:'rare'` and for **19 days pressed at the trivial-tier `+1/+1`** instead of `+3/+3`, until §DX-02g (`f229ede`) reassigned it.
>

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-11) — §DOC-02j ✅ SHIPPED `7e62f43`: a report whose own appendix says its subject was never built, and six of its seven mechanics are live code — while the table it wrote to persuade you names two monsters that have never existed.**
> `lab-report-drop-rates-balance-and-health.md` (2026-05-21), **270 → 309 lines**. Oldest remaining report by mtime, alphabetically first in the shrinking 2026-06-09 block. **Existing-Work-First:** the same uncommitted user recolor is still in the tree (HP-bar gradient / light-green playfield / theme-ink die numerals, self-labelled *"user recolor 2026-08-06"*) — **left untouched and unstaged**; this increment is docs-only. Ordering re-derived from `stat`, not from the preamble's arithmetic.
> **The headline is an inversion the program had not seen before.** Appendix A lists seven Layer-13 mechanics as *"described in this report but not yet implemented"* and **six shipped**, under the banner `` `LAYER 13: Short Rests & Necklace of Knowledge@25786` ``: the 3/day counter, the flat short-rest heal, the 2× off-inn award, the Necklace of Knowledge, bead acquisition on both rest paths, and the inventory section — with the specified bead record **byte-exact** to its field list. **Read against HEAD alone, every one of those rows reads as a live gap**, which makes this the cleanest argument in the program for the both-ways delta rule (instrument 6). The core formula survived verbatim too: `reward = floor(0.1 × AC × maxHP)` for heal and gold, all four loot sell values, all four potion heals.
> **And its worst section is a TABLE, which the transcription/narration rule says should be safe.** §II-A's five worked monster examples: **"Goblin Cutpurse" and "Orc Warlord" have 0 commits in the file's entire history**; Wererat is AC 13 against `ac:12` *at the archive as well as HEAD*; Ancient Dragon is HP 367 against `hp:546`, likewise both. One of five (Vampire Spawn 15/82) is exact. ***So the predictor is not table-versus-trace — it is whether the passage is a COPY of a source or an ILLUSTRATION composed to make a point.*** Those five were picked to display a difficulty gradient, so two were invented and two had their stats bent toward the curve being argued for. The same edit-to-persuade shows in §II-B, which transcribes `LOOT_TABLE` almost exactly (all four sell values, three of four slot counts) and **drops its Spell Scroll row**, folding those 2 slots into Minor Potions — which is precisely the 1.0 HP gap between the report's stated 28.75 HP/kill and the 27.75 its own source table yields. The live table pays **21.0**.
> **Three deltas worth keeping.** (1) **The self-funding thesis holds on two of three axes:** the heal is clamped to `hpMax` (`10 + CON mod` at level 1, `+d10 + CON mod` per level), so the heal axis **saturates** wherever `0.1 × AC × maxHP ≥ hpMax` — at level 1 that is nearly the whole pool, four of the report's own five rows are simply "full heal", and *"the dragon… heals you for 807 HP"* can never happen at any level. The engine is right; the rhetoric reads a saturating axis as a linear one. (2) **The rest architecture shipped inverted** — §III-B explicitly declines the model for the short rest (*"Rather than Hit Die rolls…"*) and HEAD does exactly that, then ships **Hit Die rolls on the long rest**, so the specified DGQR full-HP restoration never landed. (3) The quest-boost pillar is **RETIRED**, not stale (tombstone at `` `§TIMELESS-01: Stalk Helpers@38251` ``). Stale counts: 42 nodes → **416**, 8 inns → **38**, ½-HP respawn → **1 HP** (1 at the archive too, so wrong when written).
> **Filed §DX-02t** — the short rest is **implemented twice**, and the post-battle copy at `` `const _rests = () => S_story.shortRests@7133` `` spends the same allowance while withholding the bead, the token, the Action Surge recharge and the `restedAtMin` gate credit, so `quest_d0206_a3` cannot be opened by that route. **The heal arithmetic is byte-identical between the two copies**, which is the lesson: a copy that is right on the lines it duplicates and wrong in everything it omits cannot be found by diffing. **Filed §AUDIT-03aa** — *"Boy Scouts"* names two mechanics with opposite rationales and two spellings, `` `Boy Scouts Award — doubled!@25859` `` (off-inn, the spec exactly) and `` `Boyscout Night! Double rolls@36299` `` (first-visit, firing **at an inn**). **Answered §DX-02n (a)'s open design call by measurement** — `_D100_TABLE` is canonical, `LOOT_TABLE` is a d20 table its own comment miscalls d100, and **both home docs already say it was deleted and replaced by a comment stub while HEAD still declares all 20 entries**; the archive shows it unread twelve days before the commit those docs credit (§DOC-02g's rule again — a migration note is a claim about the past). **6th §AUDIT-03v/w instance, and the widest surface in the cluster** — `` `full HP recovery@35741` `` and `` `heal to full & advance a day@35740` `` are **fixed engine strings on all 38 sleep nodes**, promising a heal the dice do not deliver; unlike v/w/y(b) this is not authored content on one node, which argues the wanted detector belongs in the engine-string universe (§AUDIT-03s's premise), not in quest data.
> **Evidence:** IEEE-shaped, ~90 lines of unmeasurable design philosophy deleted and replaced by a **37-row two-way delta table** plus a re-measured worked-examples appendix; **~30 new `symbol@line` anchors**, each verified verbatim and unique *before* writing, and none placed inside a markdown table (the §DOC-02f pipe-escape hazard); `check:anchors` **419 → 456 across 17 → 18 docs, 0 dead**; `check:legacycodes` / `nodeindex` / `dupkeys` / `noderegs` / `npcregs` all **exit 0**, run individually — the `check:walk` chain was never invoked, per the standing detach-artifact rule. **Docs-only: no HTML, no tests, no §7½ — the 935/4 (940) Playwright baseline stands untouched.** **Next: §DOC-02k — `lab-report-dungeon-ten-themes.md`, then the 2026-06-09 block alphabetically.**

> **⟶ (2026-08-11) — §DOC-02e ✅ SHIPPED `3d0ab1d`: the first report in the program that was RIGHT about something HEAD still gets wrong — and the first pair of reports caught disagreeing with each other.** On "continue", took `lab-report-ceremonia-roll-skill-checks.md` (537 lines, 2026-05-26, §DESIGN-03) by the §DOC-02 ordering rule — oldest remaining by mtime, alphabetically first in the shrinking 2026-06-09 block, re-derived from `stat` as the preamble requires. Rewrote it to **308 lines**, IEEE-shaped, with the standard spec→shipped delta table (17 deltas). **Census: 50 of 61 named symbols resolve (82%); 0 of 6 node codes; all 9 quests live; all fifteen Yael prose strings verbatim.** The headline shape is **content outliving container** — 100% of the prose survives, 0% of the field names do, the exact inverse of §DOC-02b's *"the half of a document that points at code is the half that is right."* §ARCH-01 retired all nine flat `check*`/`*Award`/`complete*` fields in one pass and re-expressed the type as a `bits:[{kind:'skill_check'}]` opcode; `_rollCeremonia`'s specified 6-step body survives line-for-line inside `_resolveQuestUQF`@6962 under a different name. **7 skill-check quests specified → 2,453 at HEAD.** **Finding 1, and the reason this increment matters to the program's design:** §7 of the report specified *"show the level-up modal immediately"* on a passing roll, and **HEAD does not do it** — `_checkLevelUp()` is a mutator that advances `S_story.level` and queues the level, the pass branch never drains the queue, and `_grantExplorationXp` hard-resets it on any step onto new ground, so the player keeps the level and loses its HP roll, ASI and feature. The *failing* branch, added later by §XP-01, does it correctly three lines away. Filed **§DX-02p**; blast radius is all 2,453 skill-check quests. **Until now every delta pointed at report-rot; this one points at engine-rot, which means the delta table is a two-way instrument and the program should stop assuming HEAD is the reference.** **Finding 2:** this report and §DOC-02d's were written four days apart against the same world and **disagree on 3 of 6 node codes** — `BA` is City Streets here and the Rough Bar there. The engine resolved every `activateNode` from the prose rather than the code, so the codes were never load-bearing, only misleading. *A node code in a design doc is a citation, not an identifier.* Also filed **§AUDIT-03w** (the courier guard quotes a 20gp bribe with `onFail:[]` behind it — §AUDIT-03v's class, second instance in two increments, so it wants a detector not a third hand-filed row) and **three additions to §DX-02n** (`ceremoniaYaelAct` 5 writes/0 readers, whose five `_legacy_fn` writers are a free invariant-#4 cleanup; `skillCheckAttempts[].failures`; and **`q.xpAward` — 53 occurrences, 33 on UQF quests, 0 readers**, the undocumented twin of the self-labelled-dead `q.reward`, which moves the proposed `check:deadconsts` scope from top-level consts to **quest-entry fields**). Docs-only; no HTML edit. `check:walk` **16/16 green (exit 0)** — note the empty-output/timeout artifact recurred, and the **exit code is the authority, not the captured stdout**. **Next: §DOC-02f — `lab-report-circuit-map-theory.md`, §DOC-02c's Layer-9 sibling (expect the same retired corridor symbols; census will be fast).**

The same measurement covered the whole stack: every button in all six button-carrying blocks (KIR's three staged states, MAN/SEN/ATH's beats, the VS underground descent, the hub's hook/bind states) is panel-embedded, so **this slice ships zero verbs and says so** — hooks `alch-kir-dream`/`alch-man-noon`/`alch-sen-ship`/`alch-ath-stoic`/`wis-vs-underground`/`wis-vs-hub`, registry tail, dispatched in place. **What did leave the hooks: PDL and MLA, the stack's only button-less blocks** — 2-state pure panels, four `NODE_PANELS` entries in the DUS else-leg shape (shared DOM id, mutually exclusive `when`s, each css the state's own cssText verbatim — no fn-css needed a second time). Sibling analysis by the G-FU-b WRO rule, golden-verified: nothing co-renders afterend at PDL; MLA's only co-render is `story-ml-snake`, which sits EARLIER in the table, so table order preserves the inline LIFO (alch panel ABOVE the snake panel — pinned by a test). **One shape worth keeping: the `wis-vs-hub` hook carries the block's `VS_SHADOW→wisPage6` latch, which runs on EVERY render** — the inline latch sat before the `node.code === 'VS'` guard; `_runNodeHook` dispatches by id (`nodes` is metadata) and the dispatch call is unconditional, so the latch's reach is unchanged (G-FU-a's HW1-latch class, except this one lives inside the moved block's braces and moves WITH it; pinned by a test). **Evidence:** 37-combo golden HEAD-vs-after over sibling DOM + bounding boxes + msg text/class + pre-battle overlay/node + state (roen chain, wisdom pages, knowledge, inventory), incl. 11 click-outcome combos — **37/37 byte-identical** (the family's largest capture and its second zero-delta slice); self-stability 37/37; §7½ **8/8 story-column PNGs byte-identical**, kir-stone and vs-shadow eyeballed live; 23-authored-string sweep, every count equal to HEAD (the doubles are pre-existing QUEST_DB hints/journal prose quoting the same lines); `uqf-node-alch.test.js` **16/16 with 3 red on HEAD — exactly the registry/source tests — and all 13 behaviour tests green BOTH ways** (G2's honest shape); `uqf-node-hooks` pin 15→21; `uqf-node-hunt`'s tail pin adjusted to the contiguous run (the alch stack sits below it — the G-FU-a npc-row-pin adjustment class); gate #13 phase 6 already held `VS_SHADOW`; `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0**; 25 anchor hints refreshed by `anchors:fix` up front (the G-FU-b lesson); full Playwright **889 passed / 4 failed** (893 tests — the four are the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` set; the `multiplayer-presence:171` flake passed this run).
